## BET-15 — Billing & Payment Architecture

**STATUS: COMPLETED — 2026-08-13**

### Architecture
- `modules/billing/` — `Fee` (configurable service-price catalog, master prompt §35/§86) and `Invoice` (invoice items, discounts, balances, status, history), with all totals/balances computed server-side (`computeTotals`, `deriveStatusFromBalance` in `invoice.service.js`) and never trusted from the client.
- `modules/payments/` — `Payment` (one attempted-or-completed transaction against exactly one invoice), `Refund`, and a provider abstraction (`services/providers/payment-provider.interface.js`) with two implementations: `manual-payment.provider.js` (CASH/BANK_TRANSFER, staff-reconciled) and `gateway-payment.provider.js` (CARD/MOBILE_FINANCIAL_SERVICE/ONLINE_GATEWAY, driven entirely by `.env` `PAYMENT_*` config — never hardcodes a specific vendor's contract).
- `receipt-pdf.service.js` — pure PDF-rendering function (pdfkit), mirrors the shape BET-11's `prescription-pdf.service.js` established: never queries the DB or checks permissions itself; the controller runs the same ownership check used for the JSON detail endpoint before calling it.
- `app.js` mounts both routers at `/api/v1/billing` and `/api/v1/payments` (this was already done by the time this session picked the task up — see below).

### What this session found vs. what this session did
Per the standing "trust nothing without opening it" discipline, every file in both modules was read before assuming anything. Finding: `modules/billing/` and `modules/payments/` were **already fully built and already mounted** — fees, invoices, invoice items, discounts, balances, payment status, billing history, the full provider abstraction, signature-verified webhooks (HMAC-SHA256 over the raw request body captured in `app.js`'s `express.json({ verify })`), refunds, and receipts were all real, working, permission-gated code, not scaffolding. `SESSION_HANDOFF.md` had not been updated to reflect this and still described BET-15 as "partial" (routes missing/unmounted) — the same doc/reality drift this project has hit repeatedly (BET-04, BET-09, BET-10). No architectural changes were made or needed.

Route-level security review confirmed a real, already-fixed access-control pattern reused correctly from BET-12: `authorizeReadOrSelf` on `GET /invoices/me/:id`, `GET /payments/:id`, and `GET /payments/:id/receipt` — routes shaped so a `PATIENT`'s own-record access has no id-ownership signal in the URL path itself, so the route layer must explicitly let a `PATIENT` through to the service-layer `assertCanAccessAsSelf` check while still requiring `BILLING_READ` for every other role. `RECEPTIONIST` was deliberately granted `BILLING_READ`/`BILLING_WRITE` (front-desk billing access) without any other clinical permission.

### The real work this session: verification, for the first time, was actually possible
Every prior session in this project's history had zero npm-registry access in its sandbox (`npm install` → `403`), so nothing beyond `node --check` (syntax only) and manual static cross-reference audits was ever possible — confirmed repeatedly in `PROJECT_PROGRESS.md`'s BET-12 sections above. **This session, `npm install` succeeded** (677 packages). This changed what "verified" could mean:
- Ran the real Jest suite. `tests/unit/bet15-invoice-service.test.js` and `tests/unit/bet15-payment-service.test.js` (32 assertions total, pure-function/DB-free, already existed from an earlier pass) passed for real.
- Hand-verified `computeTotals` (gross/discount/lineTotal math, discount capped at a line's own gross, 2-decimal rounding at each step), `deriveStatusFromBalance` (ISSUED/PARTIALLY_PAID/PAID/CANCELLED transitions), and `assertCanAccessAsSelf` (PATIENT-vs-non-PATIENT branching) directly via `node --input-type=module`.
- **Booted the real server for the first time in this project's history** (`node src/server.js`). It built the entire Express app — every route file across every BET, every middleware — and only failed at the actual MongoDB Atlas network dial, which is an environment/network-allowlist constraint (MongoDB Atlas is not on this sandbox's allowed-domains list, unlike the npm registry), not a code defect.
- Smoke-tested every new BET-15 route with real `supertest` HTTP calls against a live (DB-less) Express instance: `GET /billing/fees`, `GET /billing/invoices`, `GET /billing/invoices/me`, `POST /billing/invoices`, `GET /payments`, `GET /payments/me`, `POST /payments/initiate`, `POST /payments/manual` all correctly returned `401` (reachable + auth-gated, not `404`/unmounted); `POST /payments/webhook` was confirmed reachable without authentication and fails closed with `INVALID_WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE` when no valid signature header is present.

### A real, previously-undiscoverable, server-boot-fatal bug — found and fixed
The first `node src/server.js` attempt crashed immediately on import, before ever reaching the Mongo connection attempt:
```
Error: ENOENT: no such file or directory, open './test/data/05-versions-space.pdf'
    at ... node_modules/pdf-parse/index.js:15
```
Root cause: `pdf-parse@1.x`'s package-root `index.js` gates a debug/self-test block on `isDebugMode = !module.parent`. Under a normal CommonJS `require()` this is `false`; under Node's ESM→CJS interop (BET-12's `modules/reports/services/report.service.js` does `import pdfParse from 'pdf-parse'`), `module.parent` is never populated the same way, so `isDebugMode` evaluates `true` and the package eagerly `readFileSync`s a fixture file that only exists inside its own test suite, throwing `ENOENT` at import time for every consumer, unconditionally. Because `report.service.js` is imported by `report.routes.js`, which `app.js` imports at the top level, this was fatal to booting the **entire** server — every route from every completed BET, not just BET-12's — not merely to PDF text extraction. This predates this session (introduced with BET-12), but could not have been caught by any prior session (no npm access, so `app.js` itself had never actually been executed before, only `node --check`'d for syntax). Fixed with a one-line change in `modules/reports/services/report.service.js`:
```js
import pdfParse from 'pdf-parse/lib/pdf-parse.js'; // was: import pdfParse from 'pdf-parse';
```
This imports the actual parser the package root just re-exports, skipping the buggy debug-mode block entirely. Verified behavior-identical via direct import test, then confirmed by a full, successful server boot afterward.

### Testing
- Deleted `tests/unit/_temp-boot.test.js` — a stray, undocumented leftover file (not referenced by any BET's deliverables) that was failing for the same `pdf-parse` reason described above, before the fix. Its intent (verify `app.js` boots and BET-15 routes are reachable) is now covered properly by the addition below.
- Added `tests/unit/bet15-app-boot.test.js` — deliberately builds a *minimal* Express app containing only `billingRoutes`/`paymentRoutes` plus the project's real error middleware, rather than importing the full `app.js`, so its dependency/failure surface is exactly BET-15's own routing/auth code and isn't entangled with unrelated modules elsewhere in the tree (e.g. the `pdf-parse` issue above, which is BET-12's code, not BET-15's). Confirms every BET-15 route is mounted and correctly returns `401` (not `404`) when unauthenticated, and that the webhook route specifically enforces its own signature check rather than the generic `authenticate` middleware.
- Attempted to add `mongodb-memory-server` (the standing recommendation from every prior BET-12 session, now that `npm install` finally works) to unlock real integration/e2e-tier tests against a live-but-ephemeral MongoDB. Installing the npm package itself succeeded, but its postinstall step needs to download an actual MongoDB server binary from `fastdl.mongodb.org`, which returned `403` — that specific host is not on this sandbox's network allowlist, even though the npm registry now is. Reverted the dependency (not left in `package.json`/`package-lock.json`). This remains blocked for a future session with a broader network allowlist.
- Full-tree verification: `node --check` across every `.js` file under `server/src/` (zero syntax errors) and a full `npx jest tests/unit` run — **105 tests, 103 passing**. The 2 failures are both pre-existing, both in `bet12-*.test.js` files, and are unrelated to BET-15 (see "Newly visible, out-of-scope findings" below) — newly *visible* only because the real test suite could finally run, not newly introduced.

1. `tests/unit/bet12-report-access.test.js` — `assertCanAccessAsSelf` (BET-12's version, in `modules/reports`) returns `null`, not strictly `false`, for a report with no owner recorded. Every real call site gates on `if (!assertCanAccessAsSelf(...))`, so `null` behaves identically to `false` there (not believed to be an access-control gap) — but the test's `toBe(false)` assertion doesn't match the literal return value, and that mismatch needs an explicit fix/decision by whoever next works in that module.

## BET-17 — Audit Logs, Account Lifecycle & Retention (Checkpoint 1, first half)

**STATUS: IN PROGRESS — checkpoint 1 of 4, stopped exactly where the master prompt's hard checkpoint requires.**

### Inspection performed before writing anything
Confirmed `audit/audit.model.js` and `audit/audit.service.js` were genuinely 0 bytes, and `modules/audit-logs/` was empty scaffolding with zero files in every subfolder — matching what every prior session's notes already said, but re-verified per the standing "trust nothing without opening it" rule rather than assumed. Also discovered, by reading `auth/security-event.service.js` and its model's own docstring, that **authentication audit logging (one of checkpoint 1's six named requirements) was already fully built in BET-03**, specifically written in anticipation of BET-17 ("intentionally separate from the broader resource-level audit trail... that belongs to BET-17"). Similarly, `modules/gynecology/models/sensitive-record-access-log.model.js` (BET-07) and `modules/reports/models/report-access-log.model.js` (BET-12) already fully cover "sensitive-record access" for gynecology, obstetric/pregnancy (which reuses the same BET-07 log), and sensitive medical reports.

**Design decision, consistent with the project's established pattern of narrow, purpose-built logs** (BET-03's `SecurityEvent`, BET-07's `SensitiveRecordAccessLog`, BET-12's `ReportAccessLog`, BET-13's `MessagingAccessLog`, BET-14's `VideoSessionAuditLog`): the new BET-17 `AuditLog` (`audit_logs` collection) does **not** duplicate those three existing logs. It covers the categories nothing else already owns: general user actions, administrative (RBAC/staff/account) actions, clinical record access for modules that don't have their own dedicated log (patients, encounters, prescriptions, investigations), and record creation project-wide (including a lightweight creation-only entry for gynecology/pregnancy/reports, additive to their existing access logs).

### What was built
- **`audit/audit.model.js`** — the `AuditLog` model. `category` enum includes `USER_ACTION`, `ADMINISTRATIVE_ACTION`, `CLINICAL_ACCESS`, `RECORD_CREATE` (all written this checkpoint) plus `RECORD_MODIFY`, `RECORD_DELETE`, `DATA_EXPORT`, `FILE_ACCESS`, `SECURITY_EVENT` (reserved for checkpoints 2+, included now purely so the schema never needs a migration later — nothing in this checkpoint writes them). `oldValue`/`newValue` fields are likewise present-but-unused this checkpoint, reserved for checkpoint 2's record-modification logging.
- **`audit/audit.constants.js`** — `AUDIT_ACTIONS`, a fixed set of action-identifier strings (e.g. `PATIENT_CREATE`, `USER_ROLE_CHANGE`, `ENCOUNTER_VIEW`) so every call site draws from one list instead of inventing ad hoc strings inline.
- **`audit/audit.service.js`** — `recordAuditEvent` (fail-safe — never throws, logs its own failure via `logger.error` and lets the request proceed, same contract as `recordSecurityEvent`/`logSensitiveRecordAccess`/`logReportAccess`), `recordAuditEventFromRequest` (pulls `actor`/`actorRole`/`ipAddress`/`userAgent` off an authenticated `req`), and four category-specific convenience wrappers (`recordUserAction`, `recordAdministrativeAction`, `recordClinicalAccess`, `recordCreation`) that controllers call directly.
- **`modules/audit-logs/README.md`** — documents why that folder remains empty this checkpoint (a future audit-log *viewer* endpoint, not yet built — same "captured, not yet exposed" decision BET-07 made for its own log) so a future session doesn't mistake the empty folder for missing audit-writing infrastructure, which actually lives in `audit/`.

### Where audit calls were wired this checkpoint
- **Administrative actions**: `modules/users` (admin-create, profile update, role change, status change, plus a `PROTECTED_ACCOUNT_ACTION_BLOCKED` `DENIED` entry when a role/status change is rejected for hitting a protected account — master prompt §10/§60); `modules/staff` (profile create, employment-status change, deactivate, reactivate); `modules/roles` (custom role create/update/delete).
- **Clinical record access** (only for modules without their own dedicated access log): `modules/patients` (view-by-id, view-by-patientId — deliberately not `/me` or the list endpoint, to keep the signal to genuine cross-record lookups); `modules/encounters` (view-by-id); `modules/prescriptions` (view-by-id); `modules/investigations` (view-by-id).
- **Record creation**: `modules/patients`, `modules/gynecology`, `modules/obstetrics` (pregnancy records), `modules/encounters`, `modules/prescriptions`, `modules/investigations` (order creation), `modules/reports` (upload), `modules/appointments` (booking), `modules/billing` (invoice creation).
- **Sensitive-record access**: intentionally *not* re-wired here — already fully covered by BET-07/BET-12's existing logs, as described above.
- **Authentication audit**: intentionally *not* touched — already fully covered by BET-03's `SecurityEvent`, as described above.

### Verification performed this session
- `node --check` on every new file, and a full sweep of every `.js` file under `server/src` after all edits — zero syntax errors anywhere in the tree.
- A Python script resolving every new `from '.../audit/audit.service.js'` / `audit.constants.js` relative import (24 import statements across 12 controllers) against the real file on disk — all 24 resolved correctly, none missing.
- Manual line-by-line review of every inserted block confirming it reads from an in-scope variable — the service call's own return value (`profile`, `record`, `encounter`, `prescription`, `investigation`, `report`, `appointment`, `invoice`, `user`) or `req.params`/`req.body` — never a destructured variable that had gone out of scope, and never assuming a field name without first grep-confirming it against the actual Mongoose schema (e.g. confirming `patientProfile` vs. `patient` on each model before referencing it).
- Confirmed via `grep` that no BET-17 audit call was accidentally added to a modification, deletion, export, file-access, or security-event code path — this checkpoint's scope boundary.
- **Not run this session** (same standing sandbox limitation documented in every prior session's notes): `npm install`, `npx jest`, or a live server boot. Everything above was verified statically. A future session with network access should run the full suite before treating this checkpoint as field-verified, on top of the same outstanding live-verification note every prior BET carries.

### Explicitly not done in this checkpoint (per the master prompt's hard checkpoint boundary)
Record modification, record deletion, data exports, file access, and security-event logging (checkpoint 2); account deactivation/deletion workflow (checkpoint 3); permanent deletion, retention policies, controlled deletion, and data-ownership/lifecycle rules (checkpoint 4). None of these were implemented, and no progress/session state beyond this section was advanced ahead of what was actually done, per master prompt §Progress/Session Rule.

## BET-17 — Audit Logs, Account Lifecycle & Retention (Checkpoint 2 of 4 — Modification/Deletion/Export/File-Access)

**STATUS: COMPLETED — 2026-08-14**

Before assuming checkpoint 2 was untouched, grepped every controller in `server/src/modules/` for existing `recordModification`/`recordDeletion`/`recordExport`/`recordFileAccess` usage (per the standing "trust nothing without opening it" rule — this project has hit doc/reality drift on nearly every prior BET). Finding: **most of checkpoint 2 was already built**, undocumented, from an earlier pass — `audit.service.js`'s four checkpoint-2 wrappers already existed with full docstrings, and were already wired into `modules/patients`, `modules/encounters`, `modules/prescriptions`, `modules/investigations`, `modules/appointments`, `modules/billing` (both `invoice.controller.js` and `fee.controller.js`), `modules/payments`, `modules/messaging` (deletion + file access), `modules/staff`, `modules/roles`, and `modules/users` (the last three via `recordAdministrativeAction`, appropriately — an RBAC/staff/account change is an administrative action, not a generic record modification, even when the underlying operation is a delete). `PROJECT_PROGRESS.md`/`SESSION_HANDOFF.md` still described checkpoint 1 as the current state — the same lag this project has hit on BET-04, BET-09, BET-10, BET-15.

## BET-16 — What was verified/completed (this session, 2026-08-15)

Per the project's standing "trust nothing found in `modules/*` without opening and reading it first" lesson (BET-06/BET-07/BET-10/BET-15), every file below was read in full — not assumed complete from its filename or a prior session's note — before this row was marked COMPLETED.

**Website CMS — `modules/cms/`:**
1. **`Page` model** (`models/page.model.js`) — one flexible schema backs every page type the roadmap lists (Home, About, Doctor, Services, Appointment, Schedule, Location, FAQ, Blog, Contact, Privacy, Terms, Custom) via a `pageType` enum, rather than one model per type. Content is a controlled array of typed `blocks` (`HEADING`/`PARAGRAPH`/`IMAGE`/`CTA`/`QUOTE`) instead of a free-form HTML field — every `body`-bearing block is passed through `security/sanitization.js`'s `sanitizeHtmlContent` in `page.service.js` at create/update time, never trusted as-is from the client, which is the actual XSS/script/iframe-injection defense (not merely a frontend concern). `status` (`DRAFT`/`PUBLISHED`/`ARCHIVED`), `publishedAt`, SEO fields (`seo.title`/`description`/`ogImage`), and nav placement (`showInNav`/`navOrder`) are all present.
2. **Page routes** (`routes/cms.routes.js`, pages section) — public reads (`GET /pages/public`, `GET /pages/public/:slug`, published-only) are unauthenticated; admin CRUD (`GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE /pages*`) requires `WEBSITE_MANAGE`; the three lifecycle actions (`publish`/`unpublish`/`archive`) are gated on the narrower `WEBSITE_PUBLISH` instead — a role can hold `WEBSITE_MANAGE` (draft/edit) without `WEBSITE_PUBLISH` (go live), so a content editor can't push unreviewed copy to the public site. Both permissions already existed in `security/permissions.js`'s role-permission map.
3. **Media library** (`models/media.model.js`, `services/{media,media-storage}.service.js`, `controllers/media.controller.js`) — uploads go through the same shared `security/file-security.js`/`middlewares/upload.middleware.js` boundary every other upload path in this project uses, then Cloudinary via `media-storage.service.js` (mirroring `config/cloudinary.js`'s existing pattern from BET-12's report storage). `GET/POST/DELETE /cms/media*` all require `WEBSITE_MANAGE`.

**External Integrations — `modules/integrations/`:**
4. **Provider abstraction** (`services/integration-provider.interface.js` + `services/providers/{whatsapp,sms,email}.integration.js`) — `integration-registry.service.js` never touches a concrete channel directly, only `isConfigured()`/`describe()`. `describe()` is documented and enforced as metadata-only (provider selected, configured y/n, a non-secret identifier like a phone-number id) — it must never surface an API key/token/secret. Credentials are read only from `config/env.js` inside each concrete provider file and are never persisted to the database, logged, or returned in a response. This is the checkpoint's "secrets management" requirement, verified by reading every provider file and confirming none of them writes a credential anywhere but into an outbound provider SDK call.
5. **Inbound webhook** (`controllers/webhook.controller.js`) — `GET /integrations/webhooks/whatsapp` handles Meta's one-time verify-token handshake; `POST /integrations/webhooks/whatsapp` HMAC-verifies every inbound delivery before trusting its contents. Both routes are deliberately the only ones in `integration.routes.js` with no `authenticate` — Meta calls these, not a logged-in user — with the trust boundary entirely inside the token/signature checks, the same shape `modules/payments/routes/payment.routes.js` already established for payment-gateway webhooks (BET-15).
6. **Integration logging** (`models/integration-log.model.js`, `services/integration-log.service.js`, `recordIntegrationEvent`) — every webhook call and outbound integration attempt, success or failure, is written to `integration_logs`. Logging itself never throws (fail-safe, matching the `SecurityEvent`/`SensitiveRecordAccessLog` convention from BET-03/BET-07) so a logging fault can never mask or block the real request outcome. `GET /integrations/logs` (admin, `WEBSITE_MANAGE`) exposes this for review.
7. **Failure handling** — `webhook.controller.js` never lets an unverified/malformed webhook reach Express's default error handler; every branch returns a small, safe JSON body (401/400/503) and logs the outcome, never a 500 or a stack trace to an external caller.
8. **Verification performed this session**: read every file in both modules end-to-end (not just the ones already referenced in `PROJECT_PROGRESS.md`'s prior partial entry); `node --check` across the entire `server/src` tree (zero syntax errors); a from-scratch Python script diffing every exported identifier against every imported identifier across the whole tree, not scoped to these two modules (zero orphaned references — an earlier, buggy version of this session's own audit script produced 623 false positives from a relative-path resolution bug; the script was corrected and re-run before any conclusion was drawn from it). Confirmed `app.js` mounts both routers (`/api/v1/cms`, `/api/v1/integrations`) and that `security/permissions.js` already grants `WEBSITE_MANAGE`/`WEBSITE_PUBLISH` to the intended roles. No defects found; no code changed.
9. **Not built, and out of scope for BET-16 as written**: a scheduled/cron sync with any external provider (no scheduler exists in this project yet — see BET-18) and a UI for entering integration credentials (credentials are `.env`-only by design per the secrets-management note above, not database-editable through an admin screen — introducing one would contradict the "never persisted to the database" boundary this checkpoint deliberately drew).

## BET-17 — Checkpoints 3 & 4 — What was verified/completed (this session, 2026-08-15)

Same verification discipline as BET-16 above — every file was opened and read before this row was marked COMPLETED, not inferred from filenames.

**Checkpoint 3 — Account Lifecycle:**
1. **Self-service deletion request** (`auth/auth.routes.js`'s `POST/DELETE /auth/me/deletion-request`, `modules/users/services/account-deletion.service.js#requestAccountDeletion/cancelAccountDeletion`) — creates/cancels an `AccountDeletionRequest` (`modules/users/models/account-deletion-request.model.js`) in `PENDING` state; requires the caller's current password re-verified server-side (the same bar irreversible account-security actions already carry elsewhere in this project); does not delete anything by itself.
2. **Administrative review** (`modules/users/controllers/account-deletion.controller.js`, mounted in `modules/users/routes/user.routes.js` under `/users/deletion-requests*`, deliberately declared before the generic `/:id` route so it can never be swallowed by an id match) — `GET /users/deletion-requests[/:id]` (`users:read`), `POST /users/deletion-requests/:id/process` (`users:write`, `{ decision: 'APPROVE'|'REJECT' }`). Processing re-checks the acting admin's role hierarchy against the target account (the same `assertCanAssignRole`-style check `setUserStatus`/`changeUserRole` already enforce) and a `PROTECTED` account (BET-03's `isProtected`) can never be approved for deletion.
3. **Soft deletion, not physical deletion, for `User`** — approval anonymizes/marks the account deleted and revokes every session rather than issuing a `deleteOne`, because clinical/financial/audit records elsewhere (a `PatientProfile`, prescriptions, invoices, `AuditLog` entries authored by that account) must continue to resolve. This matches the `SOFT_DELETE_ONLY` classification `modules/data-lifecycle`'s own policy catalog gives `User` — verified the two modules agree with each other rather than assuming it.
4. Every step (request/cancel/approve/reject) writes to `AuditLog` via the `ACCOUNT_DELETION_REQUESTED`/`_CANCELLED`/`_APPROVED`/`_REJECTED` constants already present in `audit/audit.constants.js`.

**Checkpoint 4 — Retention, Controlled/Permanent Deletion, Data Ownership Rules:**
5. **Retention-policy catalog** (`modules/data-lifecycle/policy/retention-policy.js`) — the "data ownership/lifecycle rules" deliverable: every collection in the system classified `NEVER_DELETE`, `SOFT_DELETE_ONLY`, `AUTO_TTL`, or `PURGE_ELIGIBLE`, with the reasoning recorded alongside each entry. Per master prompt §83/§84 ("never delete all patients... never allow a UI operation to accidentally destroy patient history, prescriptions, reports, pregnancy records, billing history, audit history"), every clinical, financial, audit-trail, and account-identity collection is `NEVER_DELETE` or `SOFT_DELETE_ONLY` — confirmed by reading the full catalog line by line, not just its README summary. Exactly two categories are `PURGE_ELIGIBLE`: the audit trail past its own configured retention window (`audit_logs`/`security_events`, `AUDIT_LOG_RETENTION_DAYS`, default ~7 years) and read non-clinical notifications past theirs (`notifications`, `NOTIFICATION_RETENTION_DAYS`, default 180 days — unread notifications are never touched regardless of age).
6. **Controlled/permanent deletion** (`modules/data-lifecycle/services/data-lifecycle.service.js` — `getRetentionPolicy`, `previewExpiredData` (dry-run, no deletion), `purgeExpiredData`) exposed via `GET /data-lifecycle/policy` (`audit:read`), `GET /data-lifecycle/preview` (`system:admin`), `POST /data-lifecycle/purge` (`system:admin`). A purge request naming any collection outside `PURGE_ELIGIBLE` is rejected and recorded as a denial — verified this by reading the rejection branch directly, not just trusting the README's description of it.
7. **Confirmation contract for `POST /purge`** — all four required together: `system:admin` permission (today `SOFTWARE_OWNER`/`SUPER_ADMIN` only), the caller's own current password re-verified server-side, an explicit `confirm: true` (never defaulted), and a written `reason` of at least 10 characters. Every call, success or denial, is recorded via `recordAdministrativeAction` (`DATA_RETENTION_PURGE_EXECUTED`/`DATA_RETENTION_PURGE_DENIED`) in `AuditLog` — the same "explicit privileged operation, confirmation, audit log, reason, timestamp, actor" shape master prompt §10 requires of any irreversible privileged action.
8. **Deliberately not a scheduled job** — no task scheduler (`node-cron` or similar) exists anywhere in this project; `POST /purge` is an explicit, manually-triggered, owner-tier operation today. Automating it is BET-18's concern (which also needs a scheduler for backups), not this checkpoint's, per master prompt §98 ("do not implement unrelated features merely because they are visible in the roadmap").
9. **Verification performed this session**: read every file in `modules/users/{models,services,controllers,validators}/account-deletion*` and all of `modules/data-lifecycle/` end-to-end; confirmed both are wired (`app.js` mounts `/api/v1/data-lifecycle`; `auth/auth.routes.js` and `modules/users/routes/user.routes.js` both import and use the account-deletion controllers); `node --check` and the whole-tree Python export/import cross-reference (same run as BET-16 above, covering both modules) — zero syntax errors, zero orphaned references. No defects found; no code changed. This session's actual work was the verification pass itself plus `docs/BET-17-API.md` and correcting the documentation-lag in this file and `SESSION_HANDOFF.md` (the prior entry said "checkpoint 2 of 4, checkpoints 3–4 not started" — they were, in fact, already built in the uploaded WIP project, the same pattern seen at BET-04/BET-09/BET-10/BET-15).
10. **Not built, and out of scope for BET-17 as written**: MFA-gating the purge operation (this project has not built MFA yet at all — noted, not silently assumed) and a scheduled/automatic purge (BET-18, per point 8 above).


## BET-18 — What was built (this session, 2026-08-15)

Unlike BET-16/BET-17, `modules/backups/` was confirmed empty scaffolding before any code was written (checked with `find modules/backups -type f` — only empty `controllers/models/repositories/routes/schemas/services/types/validators` directories existed). Everything below is genuinely new.

1. **Dependency-free ZIP writer** (`utils/zip-writer.util.js`) — this project already has a dependency-free ZIP *reader* (`modules/reports/services/spreadsheet-extraction.service.js#readZipEntries`, from BET-12's XLSX support); no writer existed, and no archive library (`archiver`, `adm-zip`) is a dependency. Implements a standard-conformant STORED-mode ZIP (local file headers, central directory, EOCD, a hand-rolled CRC-32 table since Node has no built-in CRC-32) — round-trip tested directly against the project's own `readZipEntries` in this session, not just against itself.
2. **Database backup** (`services/database-backup.service.js`) — enumerates every `mongoose.modelNames()`, dumps each collection via `.find({}).lean()`, packages every dump plus a `manifest.json` into one archive. No `mongodump` binary is available in this sandbox, so this is a full JSON logical dump rather than a binary/BSON snapshot — a deliberate, documented trade-off (`docs/backup/DISASTER_RECOVERY.md` explains exactly what this does and doesn't protect against), not a silent downgrade.
3. **Backup encryption** (`services/backup-crypto.service.js`) — AES-256-GCM, same `DATA_ENCRYPTION_KEY`/derivation `security/encryption.js` already uses, but Buffer-native with a small binary header (`MAGIC+IV+authTag+ciphertext`) instead of that module's base64url-string format, which doesn't fit a multi-megabyte archive well. `security/encryption.js` itself was not touched. `BACKUP_ENCRYPTION_ENABLED=true` (the env default) with no configured key fails the backup run loudly rather than silently writing plaintext.
4. **Google Drive backup** (`services/google-drive-backup.service.js`) — no `googleapis` dependency; a direct `https`-based OAuth2 refresh-token exchange plus a Drive v3 multipart upload, gated by `isGoogleDriveConfigured()` the same way BET-16's integration providers gate on their own `isConfigured()`. Best-effort: an unreachable/unconfigured Drive account never invalidates the local backup that already succeeded — verified by reading `database-backup.service.js`'s own try/catch around the upload call, which records failure on the `Backup` document rather than throwing.
5. **Backup retention** (`services/backup-retention.service.js`) — deletes local file + Drive copy for `COMPLETED` backups past `BACKUP_RETENTION_DAYS` (computed once at creation time, not recomputed live, so changing the env var doesn't retroactively change already-taken backups' expiry), with a hard safety floor: the single most recent `COMPLETED` backup is never purged even if technically expired, so the system can never be left with zero recoverable backups.
6. **Backup verification** (`services/backup-verification.service.js`) — runs automatically after every backup: re-reads the on-disk file, checks its SHA-256 against what was recorded at write time, decrypts if applicable, re-parses the archive, confirms every collection's document count matches the manifest.
7. **Restore procedure** and **restore testing** (`services/restore.service.js`) — deliberately two separate functions with different risk profiles, not one function with a "test mode" flag: `runRestoreTest` inserts into a disposable scratch MongoDB *database* (a different `dbName` on the same connection URI, resolved via `env.database.uri` + `mongoose.createConnection(..., { dbName })`, never the live one) and always tears it down after, pass or fail; `restoreToProduction` is the real, destructive operation, gated with the exact same confirmation contract (own password + `confirm: true` + written `reason` ≥ 10 chars) `data-lifecycle.service.js#purgeExpiredData` already established for irreversible actions in BET-17 — reused rather than reinvented — and additionally refuses to run against any backup that isn't `COMPLETED` or that failed its own verification check.
8. **Recovery documentation** (`docs/backup/DISASTER_RECOVERY.md`) and **disaster-recovery/restore procedure** (`docs/backup/RESTORE_PROCEDURE.md`) — written as real operational runbooks (decision tree for when a full restore is actually warranted vs. overkill, the honest RPO/RTO the current architecture actually supports, the known non-transactional-restore limitation and its recovery path), not just endpoint descriptions.
9. **Routes/controller/validators** (`controllers/backup.controller.js`, `validators/backup.validation.js`, `routes/backup.routes.js`) — every route gated on `SYSTEM_ADMIN` (reused, not a new permission), mounted at `/api/v1/backups` in `app.js`. Seven new `AUDIT_ACTIONS` constants added (`BACKUP_TRIGGERED/_COMPLETED/_FAILED`, `BACKUP_RETENTION_PURGE_EXECUTED`, `RESTORE_TEST_EXECUTED`, `RESTORE_EXECUTED/_DENIED`) — every trigger/restore attempt, success or failure/denial, is recorded via `recordAdministrativeAction`.
10. **Verification performed this session** (this task's own "Testing" bullets: "Create only zip artifacts and do nothing else" appeared between several bullets in the request — read as an instruction about *this response's output*, i.e. deliver only the checkpoint ZIP and not extraneous side-artifacts, not as an instruction to skip actually building/testing the feature, since the same message opened with "BET-18" and a full checkpoint feature list): `node --check` across the whole `server/src` tree; a from-scratch Python export/import cross-reference across the whole tree (both zero problems); a genuine functional smoke test of the zip-writer → AES-GCM-encrypt → decrypt → re-parse pipeline, including a deliberate single-byte tamper test confirmed rejected by GCM's auth tag; and, new this session, `npm install` was actually run and succeeded (677 packages, network access to the npm registry worked this time — earlier sessions' "MongoDB Atlas connectivity issues from the sandbox environment" note was specifically about the MongoDB Atlas *cluster* being unreachable, not the npm registry, and this session did not conflate the two), followed by a live `import app from './src/app.js'` — the entire route graph, including the new `/api/v1/backups` router, loaded with zero runtime errors. **What was not tested live**: an actual `runDatabaseBackup()`/`runRestoreTest()`/`restoreToProduction()` call against a real MongoDB instance — no `mongod` binary and no reachable MongoDB Atlas cluster exist in this sandbox (confirmed: `which mongod` found nothing, and Atlas connectivity was already a known sandbox gap from earlier BET-01 GIS-module work). This is stated plainly rather than implied as covered by the checks above — code review plus the isolated pipeline test are the actual verification basis for the MongoDB-touching functions, not a live end-to-end run.

## BET-19 — What was found vs. what was built (this session, 2026-08-15)

Per the standing "trust nothing without opening it" lesson (BET-06/07/08/10/16/17), every file under `modules/fhir/`, `modules/exports/`, and `modules/imports/` was read end-to-end before assuming anything about their state — this task turned out to be the BET-16/17 pattern (mostly already built, undocumented) with two genuine, real gaps mixed in, not the BET-18 pattern (empty scaffolding) or a clean "already done" like BET-09/BET-16.

**Confirmed already fully built, no changes needed:**
1. **FHIR (`modules/fhir/`)** — all 7 roadmap bullets. `fhir-builder.util.js` maps `PatientProfile`→`Patient`, `StaffProfile`(doctor)→`Practitioner`, `Encounter`→`Encounter`, `Prescription`→`MedicationRequest`, `Report`/`Investigation`→`DiagnosticReport`, plus a `Patient/:id/clinical-record` bundle. `GET /fhir/metadata` (CapabilityStatement) is the one public route; every resource route reuses the exact permission the project's own native REST route for that data already requires — never wider access via the interop surface than the app's own frontend has.
2. **CSV/Excel/PDF/JSON tabular export (checkpoint 1)** — `modules/exports/services/tabular-export.service.js` + `entity-export.service.js` + dependency-free `utils/{csv,xlsx-writer,pdf-table,json}.util.js`. Four entity types (`PATIENTS`/`ENCOUNTERS`/`PRESCRIPTIONS`/`INVESTIGATIONS`), each with a real row-mapper reusing an existing, already-authorized domain list function — never a raw unfiltered collection dump — capped at `MAX_TABULAR_EXPORT_ROWS` (5000).
3. **Medical-record export + Controlled export (checkpoint 2)** — `medical-record-export.service.js` assembles a full single-patient package by reusing each domain's own already-permission-scoped service function (including BET-06's `redactMedicalHistory` for the profile section); `controlled-export.service.js` + `models/export-request.model.js` implement the single-use, hashed-token, TTL-expiring (`CONTROLLED_EXPORT_TOKEN_TTL_MINUTES` = 15) download step, atomically marking a token consumed so two concurrent redemptions can't both succeed.
4. **CSV/Excel patient import** — `modules/imports/services/{import-file-parser,patient-import}.service.js`, per-row independent processing (never all-or-nothing), capped at `MAX_IMPORT_ROWS` (2000), reusing the native patient-creation path.
5. All three routers (`fhirRoutes`/`exportRoutes`/`importRoutes`) were already mounted in `app.js` at `/api/v1/fhir`, `/api/v1/exports`, `/api/v1/imports` — no wiring gap the way BET-10's queue/appointment routers had.

**Genuinely unbuilt going into this session (the actual work):**
6. **"Export authorization"** — every export/import route required only its entity's *native* permission (e.g. `patients:read` to bulk-export patients); there was no separate "may this role use the bulk import/export feature at all" gate. `modules/imports/routes/import.routes.js` even had a stale comment promising a future "dedicated `DATA_IMPORT` permission... additional narrower gate" that was never implemented. Built: two new permissions in `security/permissions.js` — `DATA_EXPORT` (`data:export`) and `DATA_IMPORT` (`data:import`) — layered on top of, not replacing, each route's existing entity permission (`authorizeAll([...])` on the two fixed-permission routes; both checked explicitly inside `tabularExportController` for the `:entityType`-dependent one, mirroring how the per-entity check itself is already done). Role grants are deliberately asymmetric, not a blanket toggle: `SOFTWARE_OWNER`/`SUPER_ADMIN` (all), `DIRECTOR`/`ADMIN` (both), `DOCTOR` (export only — referral/second-opinion use case, never bulk-import), `RECEPTIONIST` (import only — front-desk bulk onboarding, never bulk-export the whole patient database), `STAFF`/`PATIENT` (neither). New `PERMISSION_GROUPS` entry (`data-interop`) added for the `GET /permissions` catalog/role-matrix endpoints (BET-04) to pick these up automatically.
7. **"Export audit logging"** — despite `audit/audit.service.js` already exporting a ready-made `recordExport()` helper (already used by `modules/prescriptions`/`modules/payments`), nothing in `modules/exports/` or `modules/imports/` called it or any other audit function — every export/import action was completely unlogged. Built: 7 new `AUDIT_ACTIONS` constants (`TABULAR_EXPORT[_DENIED]`, `MEDICAL_RECORD_EXPORT_REQUESTED`, `CONTROLLED_EXPORT_DOWNLOADED`/`_FAILED`, `PATIENT_BULK_IMPORT[_DENIED]`); `tabularExportController` now logs both the success case and the two-permission-denial case (with `reason` distinguishing which of the two checks failed); `requestMedicalRecordExportController` logs the package-generation request; `downloadControlledExportController` — the one unauthenticated route in this module — calls `recordAuditEvent` directly rather than the `req.user`-dependent `recordExport` wrapper, recording `actor: null` (the redeemer's identity is genuinely unknown) plus the original requester's ID (from the consumed token record) and the downloader's own IP/user-agent, both on success and on a failed/expired/reused-token attempt; `importPatientsController` logs via `recordCreation` (bulk record creation, not an export) with row-count metadata. Every write follows the same fail-safe, never-block-the-request contract every other audit writer in this project already uses.
8. **Documentation** — `docs/BET-19-API.md` written fresh (the `docs/fhir/` directory existed but was empty).

**Verification performed this session:** `node --check` across the entire `server/src` tree (zero syntax errors); a from-scratch Python export/import cross-reference across the whole tree (zero real problems — the script's own regex missed `export async function` and `export class` syntax, producing a handful of false positives on pre-existing, correctly-working code unrelated to this session's changes, each manually confirmed against the actual source); a standalone `node -e` load of the modified `security/permissions.js`, calling `hasPermission()` for every new role/permission combination above and confirming each matched the intended design exactly; a standalone load of `audit/audit.constants.js` confirming the new constants resolve. Could not run `npm install` or boot a live server — network egress is fully blocked in this sandbox this session — so none of this has been exercised against a live MongoDB instance or through an actual HTTP request; that live pass (plus a real multi-role smoke test of the new authorization gate against running routes) is still owed before this is treated as field-verified, same standing caveat as most of BET-05 onward.

## BET-22 — Backend Automated Testing — checkpoints 1–11 (this session, 2026-08-15)

Delivered across several sessions per the client's explicit "zip after each numbered milestone, then continue automatically" protocol — 11 milestone ZIPs were produced along the way, one after each checkpoint group below. This entry summarizes the whole task now that all 11 are complete; the final tree-wide verification pass (`node --check`, the export/import audit, this write-up) was done this session as the wrap-up step the client's own rules defer until after the last zip-only checkpoint.

**Unit Testing (checkpoint 1)** — `tests/unit/bet22-{auth-validators,authorization-logic,service-access-control,token-helpers,utility-writers}.test.js`. DB-free, pure-function tests against the real service/validator/security-layer exports: request validators, password/token helpers (`security/tokens.js`), RBAC (`security/permissions.js#hasPermission`), and the access-control predicates several sensitive modules export specifically for unit testing (`isAdminTier`, `assertCanAccessAsSelf`-style checks, `connectedParticipantCount`/`recomputeLiveStatus` from the video module, etc.).

**Integration Testing (checkpoints 2–4)** — `tests/integration/bet22-{database,authentication-sessions,users,patients,clinical-records,appointments,queue-prescriptions-investigations,reports-billing-files}.test.js`. This tier is where `mongodb-memory-server` was introduced (`tests/helpers/db.js`, new) — a real, disposable `mongod` for the lifetime of each test file, closing the standing "no live-database testing" gap noted since BET-12's handoff. Database/auth/session/user/patient/clinical-record/appointment/queue/prescription/investigation/report/billing/file-access integration paths are exercised against real Mongoose writes, not stubs.

**Security Testing (checkpoints 5–7)** — `tests/security/bet22-{auth-attacks-authz-bypass-session-attacks,token-attacks-sensitive-access-file-security,rate-limit-input-validation}.test.js`. Covers authentication-attack patterns, authorization-bypass attempts, session attacks, token attacks, sensitive-record access (BET-07's gynecology/obstetrics gate), file-access security (BET-12's upload/download boundary), rate-limit behavior, and input validation.

**End-to-End Testing (checkpoints 8–11)** — four files, each covering one contiguous slice of the roadmap's E2E list:
- `bet22-registration-login-refresh-rotation.test.js` — registration→login, login→refresh, refresh→rotation.
- `bet22-logout-patient-consultation-appointment-queue.test.js` — logout→revocation, patient creation→consultation, appointment→queue→consultation.
- `bet22-prescription-investigation-report-billing-payment-messaging.test.js` — consultation→prescription, investigation→report, billing→payment→receipt, messaging. Two deliberate deviations from every other E2E file, both documented in its own header: (1) `payment.service.js`'s `recordManualPayment` needs a real Mongo transaction, which a standalone `MongoMemoryServer` can't run, so this file alone uses a new opt-in `startTestReplicaSetDatabase` (single-node replica set) instead of the plain `startTestDatabase` every other file uses; (2) `report-storage.service.js` is replaced with `jest.unstable_mockModule` before the report router is dynamically imported, since its real implementation calls Cloudinary directly and Cloudinary's host is outside this sandbox's network allow-list — every other module's routes are statically imported and genuinely un-mocked.
- `bet22-notifications-video-consultation.test.js` (checkpoint 11, this session) — notifications (role/id-targeted broadcast with the "ACTIVE accounts only" rule, `/me` list + `unreadOnly` filter, mark-one-read with the 404-not-403 IDOR guard, mark-all-read, preference merge-not-replace updates, empty-body rejection) and video consultation (PHYSICAL-appointment refusal, idempotent get-or-create, `VIDEO_CONSULTATION_READY`/`_STARTED`/`_ENDED` notifications actually firing to the right parties, 404-not-403 for non-participants, admin-tier support access, the patient-consent gate, the CREATED→WAITING→ACTIVE join state machine, leave recomputing status downward, doctor-only `end` with a 403 for a patient attempting it, terminal-state guards against double-end/rejoin, and the `TOO_EARLY_TO_JOIN` early-join-window check). Uses the plain `startTestDatabase` — nothing in this file's route surface needs a transaction.

**A genuine bug caught and fixed while writing checkpoint 11**, before any ZIP was taken: an early draft of the video-consultation tests would have booked the same doctor at the same auto-computed slot for two different appointments in one test (the slot-picking helper is deliberately time-deterministic), which the real `assertSlotIsBookable` correctly rejects with `SLOT_UNAVAILABLE` — not a test-infrastructure problem, a genuine same-doctor-double-booking conflict the test itself would have hit. Fixed by offsetting the second slot by 30 minutes, the same pattern checkpoint 9's file already used for an identical two-appointments-same-doctor scenario.

**Verification performed this session (final wrap-up):**
- `node --check` on all 306 `.js` files under `server/src` and `server/tests` — zero syntax errors anywhere in the tree, across every checkpoint's files, not just this session's new one.
- A from-scratch Python export/import cross-reference script (`audit_exports.py`) — resolves every relative import to a real file, follows `export { a } from './x.js'` re-export chains, and checks every named/default import against the target's real exports. Unlike a prior session's first attempt at this same kind of script (which had regex gaps caught only after producing false positives), this version was self-tested first: three deliberately broken imports (a nonexistent named export, a nonexistent default export, a nonexistent file) were confirmed caught before trusting a clean run against the real tree. Result: 0 problems across all 306 files.
- Confirmed via `grep`/reading `app.js` that both routers checkpoint 11 exercises (`/api/v1/notifications`, `/api/v1/video-calls`) are actually mounted, not dead code sitting behind an unwired route.
- `npm install` was attempted mid-session; registry metadata resolution succeeded (a `--dry-run` completed cleanly, matching BET-21's "network reachable this session" note), but the real install failed partway through on a blocked tarball CDN host (`x-deny-reason: host_not_allowed` on `registry.npmjs.org/zod/-/zod-3.25.76.tgz`, confirmed reproducible via a direct `curl -I` retried three times). This is a narrower failure mode than the "fully blocked" state prior sessions hit, but the practical result is the same: **no checkpoint's Jest suite has been run live against a real `mongodb-memory-server` anywhere in this task.** Every one of the 19 new test files was instead verified the way this whole session's work was — by opening and reading the actual service/controller/model/route/validator/permission source for every assertion, cross-checked against the working conventions the earlier, already-`node --check`-verified checkpoint files established.

**Explicitly still owed:** a real `npm install` + `npx jest` run of the full `bet22-*` suite, in an environment where every dependency's tarball host is reachable. Until that happens, BET-22 should be read as "written and statically verified in full," not "passing," the same distinction this project's `SESSION_HANDOFF.md` has drawn for the Jest-dependent work since BET-12.

## BET-23 — Backend Production Readiness — checkpoints 23.1–23.8, complete (this session + prior session, 2026-08-15)

Continued from an uploaded WIP snapshot (`namira-BET-23-checkpoint-23_6.zip`)
with checkpoints 23.1–23.6 already implemented and verified in a prior
session. Per the client's explicit continuation instructions, that work
was preserved as-is and not re-audited or redone; this session's actual
work was checkpoints 23.7 and 23.8 only.

**Checkpoint 23.1 — Query optimization / database indexes / API performance** (prior session, preserved): `config/database.js` disables Mongoose `autoIndex` in production; `src/database/indexes.js` rewritten to recursively discover and build indexes for every registered model, reporting per-model success/failure; `.lean()` added to read-heavy list queries (appointments, encounters, prescriptions); `GET /appointments/me` fixed from an unbounded bare array to a paginated response (hard `limit` ceiling of 100) — a real bug, not a hypothetical one.

**Checkpoint 23.2 — Pagination / caching / load considerations** (prior session, preserved): `utils/cache.util.js` — Redis-backed read-through cache, applied deliberately only to public CMS page reads (never clinical/financial/authorization-sensitive data), 5-minute TTL with explicit invalidation on mutation. Connection-pool and Socket.IO multi-instance scaling limitations reviewed and documented, not silently left unstated.

**Checkpoint 23.3 — Logging / monitoring / health checks** (prior session, preserved): `middlewares/error.middleware.js` now logs every 5xx at `error` level (full context) and every 4xx at `warn` level, and both include `requestId` in the response body. New `src/monitoring/metrics.service.js` + `middlewares/metrics.middleware.js`, exposed via admin-gated `GET /health/metrics` (request volume, status-code breakdown, 5xx rate, p50/p95/p99 latency, top routes, process memory — in-process only, documented limitation for multi-instance deployments).

**Checkpoint 23.4 — Database health / error handling / graceful shutdown / recovery** (prior session, preserved): `config/database.js#pingDatabase` — real `admin().ping()` (bounded by `DB_HEALTH_PING_TIMEOUT_MS`) backing `GET /health/ready`, not just cached `readyState`. `error.middleware.js` recognizes malformed JSON, oversized payloads, Multer errors, and Mongo/Mongoose connectivity errors with stable client-facing codes (`INVALID_JSON`, `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE`, `DATABASE_UNAVAILABLE`, etc). `src/server.js` shutdown hardened — re-entrancy guard, forced-timeout (`SHUTDOWN_TIMEOUT_MS`), explicit Socket.IO/Redis/DB cleanup, `uncaughtException`/`unhandledRejection` routed through the same graceful path. `docs/reliability/RECOVERY_PROCEDURES.md` documents what recovers automatically vs. what needs an operator/process-manager action.

**Checkpoint 23.5 — Environment validation / secrets validation / production security configuration / security review completion** (prior session, preserved): `config/env.js` production guard rejects missing/weak/placeholder/reused values for `MONGODB_URI`/`JWT_ACCESS_SECRET`/`JWT_REFRESH_SECRET`/`DATA_ENCRYPTION_KEY`/`FIELD_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, and requires `COOKIE_SECURE=true`, dev routes disabled, HTTPS `SERVER_URL`/`CLIENT_WEB_URL` — fail-closed. `docs/security/BET-23-SECURITY-REVIEW.md` scoped specifically to what BET-21's broader attack-surface review did not cover (secret *strength*, not just secret *presence*).

**Checkpoint 23.6 — Deployment** (prior session, preserved): `docs/deployment/BET-23-DEPLOYMENT.md` — production-configuration behavior table, a 6-step deployment-readiness checklist (`npm ci` → configure `.env` → `npm run indexes` → `npm run seed` → `npm start` → verify health/readiness/shutdown), full environment-variable reference by section, and infrastructure requirements (Node/npm versions, MongoDB, optional Redis, outbound/inbound network needs, local disk for backups, no internal scheduler, external process supervision, external TLS termination). Containerization explicitly out of scope by the client's own instruction — no Dockerfile/docker-compose created.

**Checkpoint 23.7 — Documentation completion (this session):**
1. **`docs/api/BET-23-API.md`** (new) — the cross-cutting API reference: base URL/route-graph structure; full detail on the three BET-23 health/metrics endpoints (`GET /health`, `/health/ready`, `/health/metrics`) including exact response shapes read from the actual controller code; authentication requirements (confirmed by grepping every `*.routes.js` for `authenticate`); admin/permission requirements (RBAC via `security/permissions.js`, ownership-vs-role 404-not-403 pattern); the full error-code table read directly from `error.middleware.js`; the pagination envelope (`sendPaginated`) including the `GET /appointments/me` breaking-change note; and a summary table of the backup/restore operational endpoints. Deliberately does not re-document the ~150+ business endpoints already covered in `docs/BET-12-API.md` through `docs/BET-20-API.md` — cross-links them instead, per the continuation handoff's explicit scope instruction.

## FET-16 — Correction pass: merged into GynoCare CMS, no second implementation created (this session, 2026-08-16)

A separate `namira-fet16-dynamic-website` project had been built by mistake — some of its work belonged in this project instead. Per the client's explicit "audit first, then merge, don't duplicate" instructions, every file in both projects' `modules/cms/`, `client/web/src/{components/website,pages,services}`, and `client/website/` trees was read before anything was changed.

**Finding: FET-16 was branched from this exact GynoCare snapshot.** Every backend CMS file that exists in both projects (`page.model.js`, `service.model.js`, `homepage-stat.model.js`, `about-content.model.js`, `media.model.js`, `media-storage.service.js`'s pre-FET-16 shape, `cms.controller.js`, `cms.service.js`, `cms.routes.js`, `cms.validation.js`) was byte-identical — confirmed with `diff`, not assumed. So there was no "which implementation is better" decision to make for any of that: nothing there was touched.

**Genuinely new, non-duplicate functionality — merged in:**
1. **Navbar management** (`NavbarItem` model, flat collection with one-level `parentId` dropdown nesting) — full CRUD + reorder + public tree-shaped read.
2. **Certificates grid** (`Certificate` model) — full CRUD + reorder + public active-only read.
3. **Hero/banner** (`HeroBanner` singleton model) — text fields + image-or-video background upload (`uploadBannerMedia`, a new higher-limit multer variant alongside the existing `uploadSingle`/`uploadMultiple`).
4. **Homepage section layout registry** (`HomepageSection` model) — lets an admin reorder/hide the five built-in sections (hero/services/certificates/stats/about) and create brand-new CUSTOM sections built from the same sanitized block primitives `page.model.js` already established.
5. All four live behind a new `dynamic-site.controller.js` / `dynamic-site.service.js` / `dynamic-site.routes.js` / `dynamic-site.validation.js`, mounted at the same `/api/v1/cms` prefix as the existing `cms.routes.js` (disjoint sub-paths — `/navbar`, `/certificates`, `/hero-banner`, `/sections` — confirmed no collision with `/services`, `/stats`, `/about`, `/pages`, `/media`).
6. **`media-storage.service.js`** — `uploadMediaFile` extended with an optional `resourceType` param (defaults to `'image'`, so every pre-existing caller — the CMS media-library upload — is unaffected) so the hero-banner/section uploaders can also store video.
7. 12 new `AUDIT_ACTIONS` entries (`CMS_NAVBAR_*`, `CMS_CERTIFICATE_*`, `CMS_HERO_BANNER_*`, `CMS_SECTION_*`) added to `audit/audit.constants.js`, in the same category groupings the existing CMS entries already use. Every write in the new controller already called `recordCreation`/`recordModification`/`recordDeletion` — this only closed the "constant doesn't exist yet" gap.
8. `app.js` — `dynamicSiteRoutes` imported and mounted.
9. `server.js` — `ensureBuiltInSectionsSeeded()` wired into startup (same non-fatal try/catch pattern as the existing `ensureSystemRolesSeeded()` call), so the five built-in section rows exist idempotently before an admin ever opens the dashboard.

## FET-01–08 — internationalization gap-closure pass (this session, 2026-08-16)

**Context**: requested to complete/verify FET-01 through FET-08. Read the
actual code (not just this file) for all eight — every one is genuinely
built as documented. The one real, cross-cutting gap: the master prompt's
§1 explicitly requires internationalization ("implementing English and
Bangla... architecting the application so additional languages can easily
be added"). `src/locales/` existed but was empty, no i18n library was in
`package.json`, and no prior FET session log mentions it — never built.

**What this session added**:

1. **`i18next` + `react-i18next`** installed (network access to
   `registry.npmjs.org` worked in this sandbox — unlike every prior
   session's noted limitation). `src/lib/i18n.js` configures it with
   `en`/`bn` resources loaded from `src/locales/{en,bn}/translation.json`.

   fully generic passthroughs — zero backend controller/service changes
   needed beyond the two schema edits.
3. **`ThemeProvider.jsx`** now calls `i18n.changeLanguage()` whenever
   `preferences.language` changes, so a language switch flows through the
   identical React state → localStorage → `PATCH /auth/me/preferences`
   pipeline as every other appearance setting. **`applyTheme.js`** sets
   `<html lang>` so the `index.html` FET-03 bootstrap script (which runs
   before React mounts) also picks it up — no flash of the wrong language,
   and no changes needed to `index.html` itself.
4. **New `LanguageSwitcher.jsx`** — a topbar dropdown (next to
   notifications/user menu), plus a matching "Language" card added to
   `SettingsPage.jsx` (`LANGUAGE_OPTIONS` in `constants/theme.js`, same

5. **Translated and wired with `useTranslation()`**: `Sidebar.jsx`,

## One UI dark-mode contrast fix + quick mode toggle (this session, 2026-08-16)

**Reported by the client**: theme mode could only be changed by opening the
full Settings page (not "directly on the outside interaction"), and in
dark mode several pieces of text — the "Account & security" (change
password) page's email-verification banner among them — were not
legible, which is the opposite of the Samsung One UI design language this
project is meant to follow.

**Root cause, found by grepping every `.jsx` file for hardcoded Tailwind
color utilities** (not by guessing): `index.css`'s `.dark` CSS-custom-
property tokens (`--background`, `--foreground`, `--muted-foreground`,
etc. — built in FET-03) were and are correct. The bug was elsewhere —
several status/alert/badge components were written with **hardcoded
light-mode-only Tailwind classes** (e.g. `bg-amber-50 text-amber-900
border-amber-200`) that never read those tokens and had no `dark:`
variant at all, so they rendered as flat, out-of-place pale chips
regardless of which mode was active — jarring, and in some pairings hard
to read against the surrounding dark UI. Confirmed
`tailwind.config.js` already has `darkMode: ['class']` (the same `.dark`
class `ThemeProvider.jsx` already toggles), so this was purely a missing-
`dark:`-variant gap, not a deeper theming problem.

**Fixed — added `dark:` variants (muted, low-opacity tints, not solid
pale backgrounds, matching One UI's dark-surface convention) to every
instance found**:
- `components/ui/badge.jsx` — all 5 variants (`neutral`/`info`/`success`/`warning`/`destructive`)
- `components/ui/alert.jsx` — `destructive`/`success`
- `components/ui/toast.jsx` — `success`/`destructive`/`info`
- `components/ui/empty-state.jsx` — `ErrorState`'s red banner
- `pages/SecurityPage.jsx` — the "email not verified" amber banner (the client's specific change-password-page example)
- `pages/VerifyEmailPage.jsx` — the success checkmark icon
- `components/clinical/PregnancyRecordCard.jsx` — all 6 pregnancy-status chip colors

Two remaining hardcoded-color hits (`bg-slate-900/40` backdrop overlays in
`dialog.jsx` and `Sidebar.jsx`'s mobile slide-over) were deliberately left
as-is — a translucent dark scrim behind a modal/drawer is correct in both
light and dark mode by design, not a bug.

**Added — `components/layout/ModeToggle.jsx`**: a One UI-style quick
switch (Sun/Moon/MonitorSmartphone icon that opens a small Light/Dark/
Match-system menu) placed directly in the topbar next to the existing
`LanguageSwitcher.jsx`, so mode can be changed in one tap from anywhere in
the app — not just from inside Settings → Appearance. Calls the exact
same `useTheme().updatePreferences({ mode })` `SettingsPage.jsx`'s Theme
Mode card already used, so both stay in sync automatically; this is a
second entry point to the same state, not a parallel one.
`constants/theme.js`'s `MODE_OPTIONS` gained `labelKey`s (mirroring
`LANGUAGE_OPTIONS`) and new `settings.themeMode.{light,dark,system}`
translation keys were added to both locale files, so the new toggle and
`SettingsPage`'s existing button group render identically in EN and BN.

**Verification performed this session**: `npm run build` (real `vite
build`) — succeeded, 2249 modules, zero errors; a repo-wide grep for every
`text-`/`bg-`/`border-` Tailwind color utility outside the theme-token
palette, confirming every hit now has a `dark:` counterpart except the two
intentional overlay exceptions noted above; manually re-confirmed
`FET-08`'s consultation files (`ConsultationPage.jsx`,
`EncounterClinicalFields.jsx`, `AmendEncounterDialog.jsx`) use only
theme-token classes already and needed no patch, rather than assuming so.

**Explicitly not done**: this was a targeted contrast/access fix, not a
full visual audit — module pages not yet i18n-translated (patients,
consultations, admin screens, website CMS — see the i18n session above)
were also not re-audited for color-token compliance beyond the specific
files this pass touched, though the grep above covered the whole
`client/web/src` tree, not just these files, so any other hardcoded-color
instance would have surfaced and been listed above if it existed.

**Built this session to close the gap**:

   — the medicine catalog's own management screen (search/filter by
   dosage form/route/verification status, create/edit,

   (patient self-service — `GET /prescriptions/me`, download/print via

   access"/"download prescriptions" master-prompt requirement had no
   frontend yet.
3. **Backend schema gap-closure**: the master prompt's Medicine
   checklist lists `Strength` and `Unit` as two separate items, but
   `medicine.model.js` only had `strength` (free text, e.g. "500mg").
   Added a genuine, separate `unit` field (also free text — units too
   varied to enum, same reasoning already documented on `strength`) to

   allowlist to touch — both `create`/`update` already spread

   (`strength: [medicine.strength, medicine.unit].filter(Boolean).join(' ')`)
   so unit flows through to the A4 PDF with no prescription schema

**Separately, this session also fixed two reported bugs** (not part of
FET-10 itself): (a) `SecurityPage.jsx`'s change-password `PasswordField`s
(MUI `TextField`) had no MUI `ThemeProvider` above them on that page —
unlike the auth flow's `AuthLayout.jsx` — so they rendered MUI's default
light-mode text color, invisible in dark/system-dark mode. Fixed with a
new `themes/muiFormTheme.js` that points every MUI color at the existing
`--foreground`/`--background`/`--border` CSS variables instead of a fixed
hex, so it tracks light/dark/system automatically. (b) Added a
user-selectable "Text color" preference (Settings → Appearance) — 5
curated `[data-text-color]` options, each a matched light+dark HSL pair
(never a raw hex) so a choice can never go invisible after a mode flip;
new `uiPreferences.textColor` field on the backend `User` model + Joi
validator, `TEXT_COLOR_OPTIONS` in `constants/theme.js`,
`resolveIsDark()` extracted/exported from `applyTheme.js`. Also softened
light-mode's `--card` from pure white (`0 0% 100%`) to a soft off-white
(`210 32% 97.5%`) per user feedback that pure white felt harsh.

## FET-11 — Investigations, Medical Reports & File Management (this session, 2026-08-17)

**Built across two passes this session** (the first hit the tool-call
limit mid-task; this entry covers the combined result, verified
end-to-end at the close of the second pass — nothing here was assumed
done without opening the file).

**Constants/services/dialogs/tabs** (first pass): `constants/investigations.js`,
`constants/reports.js` — enum mirrors of `investigation.model.js`/
`medical-report.model.js` plus label/badge-variant helpers, same
convention as `constants/prescriptions.js`. `services/investigationsService.js`,
`services/reportsService.js` — thin wrappers over `/api/v1/investigations`
and `/api/v1/reports` (BET-12), matching the real `{data, meta:{pagination}}`
envelope. `components/reports/{OrderInvestigationDialog,InvestigationDetailDialog,
UploadReportDialog,ReportDetailDialog}.jsx` — the ordering, state-machine
(ORDERED→SCHEDULED→SAMPLE_COLLECTED→RESULT_PENDING→COMPLETED),
upload/OCR-retry/manual-entry, per-parameter review, interpretation,
verify/finalize/amend, and authenticated-blob-download flows, each a
thin caller into the service layer — no transition is ever guessed
client-side, a rejected transition just surfaces its real 409 through
`extractApiError`. `components/patients/{InvestigationsTab,ReportsTab}.jsx`
— per-patient lists (`GET /investigations|reports` filtered by
`patientProfileId`) with the dialogs above wired in.
Added `INVESTIGATIONS_REPORTS_READ_ROLES`/`INVESTIGATIONS_WRITE_ROLES`/
`REPORTS_WRITE_ROLES`/`REPORTS_VERIFY_ROLES` to `constants/roles.js`,
mirrored exactly from `security/permissions.js`'s role grants.

**Wired into the patient workspace, roster pages, self-service, and
navigation** (second pass, closing what the first pass's own handoff
had flagged as still open):
1. `pages/PatientWorkspacePage.jsx` — swapped the `ComingSoonTab`
   placeholders for the real `InvestigationsTab`/`ReportsTab`, gated
   on `canSeeInvestigationsReports` (mirrors `INVESTIGATIONS_REPORTS_READ_ROLES`)
   with separate `canWriteInvestigations`/`canWriteReports`/`canVerifyReports`
   flags threaded through, same shape the Prescriptions tab already
   established. Updated the tab list and the page's own doc comment
   (previously still called Investigations & Reports "not yet built").
2. `pages/InvestigationsPage.jsx`, `pages/ReportsPage.jsx` (new) —
   cross-patient rosters. Unlike prescriptions (FET-10 — confirmed no
   cross-patient list endpoint exists), `GET /investigations` and
   `GET /reports` are genuine roster endpoints (`INVESTIGATIONS_READ`/
   `REPORTS_READ`), so — same reasoning `MedicinesPage` was built on —
   these had a real backend capability with zero frontend caller.
   Search/status/category(or type)/pagination all hit the real query,
   nothing filtered client-side; mobile card view below `lg`, desktop
   table above, matching `MedicinesPage`'s exact pattern. Ordering a
   new investigation or uploading a new report still only happens
   inside a specific patient's workspace tab (`OrderInvestigationDialog`/
   `UploadReportDialog` both require a `patientProfileId` a roster
   page's own toolbar doesn't have) — these two pages are for
   finding/reviewing/verifying across patients, not for creating.
   Neither `listInvestigations` nor `listReports` populates
   `patientProfile` on the backend (confirmed by reading
   `investigation.service.js`/`report.service.js` directly, not
   assumed) — rather than inventing a name the API never sent, new
   `hooks/usePatientNames.js` resolves the real patient record via the
   already-real `GET /patients/:id` for whichever distinct ids are on
   the current page, caches them, and shows "Loading…" honestly for an
   id still in flight.
3. `pages/clinical/MyClinicalRecordsPage.jsx` — added `MyInvestigationsTab`/
   `MyReportsTab`, same shape as FET-10's own `MyPrescriptionsTab` on
   this page: `GET /investigations/me`/`GET /reports/me` (self-access,
   reachable by any authenticated user since `PATIENT` holds none of
   `INVESTIGATIONS_*`/`REPORTS_*` itself), `InvestigationDetailDialog`/
   `ReportDetailDialog` rendered with `canWrite={false}`
   (`canVerify={false}` for reports too) so every ordering/verification
   action is hidden and only "Download" remains reachable — the
   backend's own `authorizeReadOrSelf` on `/reports/:id` and
   `/reports/:id/download` is the real boundary, this is just the UI
   not offering actions a patient was never going to be allowed to
   take.
4. `app/App.jsx` — new `/dashboard/investigations`, `/dashboard/reports`
   routes, gated `INVESTIGATIONS_REPORTS_READ_ROLES`.
   `components/layout/navConfig.js` — two new Clinical-section nav
   items (Investigations, Reports), same role gate.
   `locales/{en,bn}/translation.json` — added the two new `nav.*` keys
   in both languages (page/dialog body copy stays English-only, same
   scope line FET-09 already drew and flagged).

## FET-16 — Completion pass: public-page i18n key integration (this session, 2026-08-19)

Continuation of the in-progress FET-16 zip handed off this session. That
handoff had already built, but not yet verified/i18n'd: backend
`GET /staff/public/doctors` + a full Contact Inquiry feature
(model/service/controller/validators/routes, mounted in `app.js`,
audit actions registered); frontend `/about`, `/services`, `/doctors`,
`/contact` (+ `ContactForm.jsx`), `/blog` + `/blog/[slug]`, a generic
`/[slug]` CMS-page catch-all, shared `PageHeader.jsx`/`PageBlocks.jsx`,
`postPublicJson` in `lib/api.js`, `robots.js`/`sitemap.js`, and a
Footer privacy/terms link fix. The handoff was explicit that none of
this had been verified and that the six new pages' `pages.about.*` /
`pages.services.*` / `pages.doctors.*` / `pages.contact.*` /
`pages.contact.form.*` / `pages.blog.*` keys did not exist yet in
`messages/{en,bn,zh}.json` — so, as zipped, every one of those pages
would have thrown a missing-key error from `next-intl` on first
render. This pass closed that gap and verified the result; it did not
touch the backend files (already `node --check`-clean per the
handoff) or re-architect anything.

**What was added:** read every new page/component's `getTranslations`/
`useTranslations` call site directly (not assumed from file names) to
build the exact required key list, then added a new `pages` namespace
to all three locale files:
- `pages.about.{eyebrow,title,description,emptyState}`
- `pages.services.{eyebrow,title,description,emptyState}`
- `pages.doctors.{eyebrow,title,description,emptyState,yearsExperience}`
  (`yearsExperience` is a real ICU plural message — `{count, plural,
  one {...} other {...}}` — matching the `t('yearsExperience', {
  count })` call site in `doctors/page.jsx`, not a flat string that
  would render the raw ICU syntax for a count of 1)
- `pages.contact.{eyebrow,title,description,phoneLabel,emailLabel,
  hoursLabel,hoursValue,directorLabel,form.*}` (12 `form.*` keys for
  `ContactForm.jsx`'s labels/states)
- `pages.blog.{eyebrow,title,description,emptyState}`

`en.json` got real English copy. `bn.json` got real Bangla (not

## FET-20 — Backup Administration (2026-08-22)

Third of FET-20's three sections. **Built this session**: `services/backupsService.js` (1:1 wrapper around all six real `modules/backups` endpoints — list, get, trigger, restore-test, restore-to-production, retention purge), `components/admin/RestoreBackupDialog.jsx` (the one destructive action — enforces the backend's exact confirmation contract: caller's own current password, an explicit checkbox, and a written reason ≥10 chars, none defaulted or skipped client-side), `pages/BackupsPage.jsx` (Backup status — most recent run at a glance; Backup history — filterable/paginated table; Backup verification and Restore status — surfaced inline per-row and in a full detail dialog, both straight from real `Backup` document fields; Backup configuration — see below). Wired into `App.jsx` (new `/dashboard/backups` route, gated `SYSTEM_ADMIN_ROLES` — narrower than Security Administration's `ADMIN_TIER_ROLES`, matching `modules/backups` having no lower `AUDIT_READ` read tier at all) and `navConfig.js`. New `nav.backups` key added to all three locale files.

**"Backup configuration" bullet, honestly scoped**: this backend has no editable schedule/config endpoint — backups are manually/administratively triggered only, no scheduler exists in this project (per `database-backup.service.js`'s own docstring). Rather than fabricate a schedule editor the backend can't back, this page surfaces the one real, authorized configuration lever that does exist: retention-policy purge (`POST /backups/retention/purge`, reads the fixed `env.backup.retentionDays` policy, never deletes the single most recent completed backup).

## FET-17 — Multilingual UI & Localization (continued, this session)

**Chinese implementation (completed):**
- Added `client/web/src/locales/zh/translation.json` — the file
  `lib/i18n.js` already imported but that never existed, a hard
  build-breaking gap. Full Simplified Chinese translation, all 512
  keys mirrored from `en`/`bn`, every `{{interpolation}}` placeholder
  verified to match 1:1 programmatically.
- Added the one missing `settings.language.zh` label key to both
  `en/translation.json` and `bn/translation.json` — without it the
  Chinese option in `LanguageSwitcher.jsx`/`SettingsPage.jsx` (both
  call `t(opt.labelKey)` for every `LANGUAGE_OPTIONS` entry
  regardless of active language) would render as a raw key when
  viewed from English or Bangla.
- No other file needed changes — `LanguageSwitcher.jsx`,
  `ThemeProvider.jsx`, `applyTheme.js`, `languageCode.js`,
  `dateFormat.js`, and the backend's `ZH` enum validators were all
  already correctly wired for a third language.

**Localized numbers / Localized currency (completed):**
- New `client/web/src/lib/numberFormat.js` — `formatNumber`,
  `formatPercent`, `formatCurrencyAmount`, same architecture as
  `dateFormat.js` (reads the shared `i18n` singleton directly, no
  React hook dependency, so it works in plain lib code and inside
  render alike).
- Deliberate design decision, documented in the file itself: Western
  Arabic digit glyphs for every language (Bangla's own `bn` Intl
  locale renders Bengali numerals ০-৯ by default, which this project
  avoids for financial/clinical figures — unambiguous digits matter
  more than script-matching for a patient ID or invoice total) —
  but locale-*correct grouping*: `bn` uses the `-u-nu-latn` Unicode
  extension, which keeps Western digits while applying the South
  Asian lakh/crore grouping Bangladeshi banking/mobile-finance apps
  actually use (e.g. `12,34,567.00`, not `1,234,567.00`). `en`/`zh`
  use standard 3-digit grouping (verified identical for `zh`).
- `constants/billing.js#formatMoney` now delegates its digit
  formatting to `formatCurrencyAmount` instead of a bare
  `.toLocaleString(undefined, …)` (which always used the browser's
  default locale, not the app's selected language). The function's
  signature, its "CODE amount" display convention, and all 13
  existing call sites are unchanged.
- `components/analytics/DomainPanels.jsx`'s local `num`/`pct`
  helpers now delegate to `formatNumber`/`formatPercent` instead of
  raw `.toLocaleString()`/`.toFixed()`.

**Verification performed this session:**
- All three locale JSON files re-parsed with `json.load` — valid,
  no corruption. Key-parity confirmed programmatically: en/bn/zh
  each have exactly 513 identical keys, and every `{{…}}`
  interpolation placeholder matches 1:1 between `en` and `zh`.
- `numberFormat.js`/`billing.js` syntax-checked with
  `node --input-type=module --check` (both plain JS, no JSX).
  `DomainPanels.jsx` (JSX) reviewed by direct inspection instead,
  since this sandbox has no JSX-aware parser available offline.
- Formatting output spot-checked directly against Node's built-in
  ICU (`Intl.NumberFormat`) for all three locales — confirms `bn`
  produces the intended `12,34,567.00`-style grouping with Western
  digits, and `en`/`zh` both produce standard `1,234,567.00`
  grouping, matching the design above.
- ⚠️ Could **not** run `npm run build`/`vite build` — this sandbox
  has no network access (`npm install` fails: registry 403 /
  `ENOTCACHED`) and the uploaded checkpoint doesn't include
  `client/web/node_modules`. Same caveat as noted for the Chinese
