## FET-18 — Text-to-Speech & Accessibility Interaction — 2026-08-21

Per the master prompt's roadmap order, this was the next unstarted FET. Before writing anything, inspected `modules/*`-equivalent frontend locations for FET-18 the same "read before trusting" way every prior session has had to (BET-04/06/07/10/12/15 all found the file tree lying about actual state at least once). This time the finding was the opposite of the usual trap: **FET-18 was already fully built**, just never given its own dated section in this file — the same documentation-lag pattern BET-04/BET-09/BET-16/BET-17 hit, now confirmed a fourth time on the frontend side.

### What was found (read end-to-end, not assumed)

- `client/web/src/lib/speech.js` — a dependency-free wrapper over the native Web Speech API (`speechSynthesis`/`SpeechSynthesisUtterance`). Handles Chrome's async `getVoices()` quirk (resolves once real voices are available via the `voiceschanged` event, with a 1s timeout fallback so a browser that never fires the event doesn't hang forever), a language-aware voice picker (exact BCP-47 match → same-family prefix match → `null`/browser-default), a clamped utterance builder, and a `cancelSpeech()` escape hatch.
- `client/web/src/hooks/useTextToSpeech.js` — the React layer. Deliberately models the single browser-global `speechSynthesis` queue as single shared state (calling `speak()` always cancels whatever was previously speaking, so two components on the same page can't talk over each other), and stops any in-flight speech on unmount so navigating away mid-sentence doesn't leave the browser talking over the next page with no visible control to stop it.
- `client/web/src/hooks/useTextSelection.js` — global `document`-level `selectionchange` tracking (text + viewport-relative bounding rect), deliberately not scoped to a single ref so "read selected content" works from inside any card/table/dialog across the app. Explicitly ignores selections inside `INPUT`/`TEXTAREA` (read-aloud is for content, not text someone is actively editing).
- `client/web/src/providers/SpeechProvider.jsx` — one shared TTS session for the whole app (same "one provider, everyone reads through `useX()`" shape as `ThemeProvider`), layered on `useTextToSpeech`. Its `speakText()` is the one entry point every consumer uses — it applies the user's persisted speech preferences (rate/pitch/volume/voice, the `speech.enabled` kill switch) and the app's *current* language automatically, so no caller has to re-derive any of that. `readMainContent()` deliberately targets `<main>` (the element `AppShell.jsx` always renders around every dashboard route's `<Outlet/>`) rather than `document.body`, so sidebar/topbar chrome and open toasts aren't read aloud along with the actual page content. Correctly mounted in `main.jsx`'s provider tree (inside `ThemeProvider`, since it reads `preferences.speech`; outside `ToastProvider`/`ConfirmProvider`).
- **Voice/read controls** — three real components, all correctly wired:
  - `ReadPageButton.jsx` — topbar entry point (mounted in `Topbar.jsx`), reads the current route's main content aloud; hidden entirely (not just disabled) when the browser has no speech support or the user turned the feature off in Settings.
  - `ReadSelectionButton.jsx` — floats above any live text selection anywhere in the app (portal to `document.body`, mounted once in `AppShell.jsx`, same pattern as `ToastProvider`/`ConfirmProvider`); reads that selection aloud, turns into a Stop button while speaking.
  - `SpeechControlsBar.jsx` — a persistent bottom-centered dock (portal, mounted once in `AppShell.jsx`) that appears the instant anything is being read aloud and disappears the instant it ends, with pause/resume/stop.
- **Speech settings** — `SettingsPage.jsx` has a full, real Speech card: enable/disable toggle, rate/pitch/volume sliders, a voice picker filtered to voices plausibly matching the app's current language (falls back to the full list if none match), and a "Test voice" button that speaks `t('settings.speech.testPhrase')` through the live `speakText()`. Every control calls `updateSpeechPref()`, which merges into `preferences.speech` (never clobbering sibling fields) through the same `updatePreferences` → localStorage → debounced `PATCH /auth/me/preferences` pipeline every other Settings control on this page already uses.
- **User preference persistence — real, not client-only.** `server/src/users/user.model.js`'s `uiPreferencesSchema.speech` subdocument (`enabled`/`rate`/`pitch`/`volume`/`voiceName`, with matching min/max bounds) and `server/src/auth/auth.validation.js`'s `updatePreferencesSchema.speech` Joi schema mirror each other exactly, and `auth.service.js#updateUserPreferences` merges partial `uiPreferences` updates non-destructively (spreads the existing subdocument before applying `updates`), so a rate-only change from the slider never wipes a previously-chosen voice.
- **Language-aware speech.** `SpeechProvider.speakText()` maps the app's active `preferences.language` (`EN`/`BN`/`ZH`) to a BCP-47 code (`en-US`/`bn-BD`/`zh-CN`) and passes it to every `speak()` call unless a caller explicitly overrides it — so "read this page" and "read selection" both speak in whatever voice matches the language the UI itself is currently in, not a fixed default.

None of this needed to be built — it was all real, correct, and already reachable in the running app. What was genuinely missing:

### Closed this session — the one real gap: `bn`/`zh` locale coverage

`accessibility.speech.*` (8 keys) and `settings.speech.*` (13 keys) — 21 keys total — existed only in `en/translation.json`. This was already flagged as a known, undecided gap by two prior FET-17 checkpoints in this same file (see above); this session (FET-18 itself) is the natural place to close it, since FET-18 is the feature these keys actually belong to. Added real Bangla and real Chinese translations for all 21 keys (not staged English) — e.g. `settings.speech.title` → বাংলায় "স্পিচ" / 中文 "语音朗读"; `accessibility.speech.readPage` → "এই পৃষ্ঠাটি পড়ুন" / "朗读本页" — matching this project's established tone in the surrounding `settings.*`/`accessibility.*` groups (direct, second-person, no literal machine-translation phrasing).

### Verification performed this session

- All three `translation.json` files re-parsed with `json.load` — valid, no corruption.
- Key-set parity checked programmatically across all three locales: **1227 keys each in `en`/`bn`/`zh`, identical sets, zero drift in either direction** — this closes the 21-key gap every FET-17 checkpoint this session had been carrying forward unresolved.
- Every `{{interpolation}}` placeholder cross-checked across all three locales for every key — 0 mismatches (moot for these particular 21 keys, none of which interpolate, but re-run tree-wide as a full regression check, not narrowed to just the new keys).
- A whole-`src`-tree sweep for every static `t('...')` call (same script prior sessions have used, plural-suffix-aware): **1127 keys checked, 0 missing** anywhere in the frontend.
- Per-file `esbuild --jsx=automatic` transform check (fresh `npm install esbuild --no-save` in a scratch dir — this session has live npm registry access) on all speech-related files: `lib/speech.js`, `hooks/useTextToSpeech.js`, `hooks/useTextSelection.js`, `providers/SpeechProvider.jsx`, `components/accessibility/{ReadPageButton,ReadSelectionButton,SpeechControlsBar}.jsx`, `pages/SettingsPage.jsx`, `constants/theme.js`, `main.jsx` — **0 errors**.
- Full `esbuild --bundle` from `app/App.jsx` through the real module graph (every real dependency `--external`, 699-byte externals list generated straight from `package.json`): resolves clean, same 3 pre-existing `import.meta`/iife warnings every prior session has flagged (harmless — `apiClient.js`/`systemHealthService.js`/`socketService.js` reading `import.meta.env`, expected under esbuild's `iife` output format; Vite's real `esm` build doesn't hit this), **0 errors**.
- Confirmed via direct read (not grep-only) that `ReadPageButton` is mounted in `Topbar.jsx`, `ReadSelectionButton`/`SpeechControlsBar` are mounted in `AppShell.jsx`, and `SpeechProvider` is mounted in `main.jsx`'s provider tree in the correct position (inside `ThemeProvider`, since it depends on `preferences.speech`) — the exact "routes exist AND are mounted" check the Known Issues section below has flagged as the recurring failure mode in this project (BET-10/BET-15's routes-not-mounted bugs), applied here to a frontend provider/mount-point instead of a backend route.

- Read selected content ✅ (`useTextSelection.js` + `ReadSelectionButton.jsx`)

All seven roadmap bullets for FET-18 are genuinely satisfied. **FET-18 is complete.**

## FET-20 — Audit Administration, checkpoints 1–4 of 4 — COMPLETE (2026-08-22)

Final of FET-20's three sections (Security Administration and Backup Administration were completed in their own earlier dated sessions above). Delivered across four client-authorized checkpoints, each zipped and shipped separately per the client's own zip-checkpoint protocol, `PROJECT_PROGRESS.md`/`SESSION_HANDOFF.md` deliberately left untouched at each individual checkpoint per the client's explicit instruction — this section is the single consolidated write-up for all four, done now that the whole feature is complete.

**Checkpoint 1 — Audit log, User activity, Administrative activity.** All three backed by the already-complete `modules/audit-logs` admin viewer (`GET /api/v1/audit-logs`, `AUDIT_READ`-gated), confirmed by direct read of `audit-log.controller.js`/`audit-log.routes.js`/`audit-log-query.service.js` before writing anything — zero backend changes needed. `category` was already a filterable field, and `USER_ACTION`/`ADMINISTRATIVE_ACTION` were already two of the real `AUDIT_CATEGORIES` values (`server/src/audit/audit.model.js`), so "User activity"/"Administrative activity" are simply this one endpoint with `category` fixed per tab — the same pattern `SecurityEventsSection.jsx` already established for "Security events" vs "Authentication events". Built: `services/auditAdminService.js`, `pages/AuditAdminPage.jsx` (three-tab table), wired into `App.jsx` (`/dashboard/audit-admin`, `ADMIN_TIER_ROLES`, mirroring `AUDIT_READ`) and `navConfig.js` + all three locale files (`nav.auditAdmin`, verified 1231/1231/1231 key parity, zero drift).

**Checkpoint 2 — Clinical record access, Sensitive-record access, File access.** "Clinical record access" needed no new backend — the same general endpoint, `category: 'CLINICAL_ACCESS'`. "Sensitive-record access" and "File access" each required a new, narrowly-scoped admin-facing read route: `SensitiveRecordAccessLog` (BET-07) and `ReportAccessLog` (BET-12.24, literally named "File Access Audit" in its own model docstring) were both existing, already-complete, already-being-written-to collections with no admin viewer until this checkpoint. Added `GET /audit-logs/sensitive-record-access` and `GET /audit-logs/file-access` — both `AUDIT_READ`-gated identically to the existing route, both registered before the trailing `/:id` param route so the literal paths aren't swallowed by it. No new writer anywhere — pure read-only viewers over data that was already being logged. The checkpoint-1 table was extracted into a reusable `components/audit-admin/AuditLogSection.jsx` (now serves four category-parameterized tabs instead of duplicating the table), plus two new dedicated components, `SensitiveRecordAccessSection.jsx`/`FileAccessSection.jsx`, since those two collections' outcome vocabulary (ALLOWED/DENIED plus a `denialReason`) differs from the general trail's SUCCESS/FAILURE/DENIED.

**Checkpoint 3 — Data exports, Security events, Filters.** "Data exports" — same general endpoint again, `category: 'DATA_EXPORT'`, no new backend. "Security events" — no new backend, no new component: `SecurityEventsSection.jsx` from Security Administration is reused wholesale as a sixth (later eighth) tab rather than duplicated, since it already covers both "Security events" and "Authentication events" over the one real event log. "Filters" — added outcome + date-range controls (`FilterBar` + the project's established `selectClass`/paired-date-input convention, matching `SecurityEventsSection.jsx`/`AnalyticsFilterBar.jsx`) to all three sections built so far. All three filter parameters (`outcome`/`from`/`to`) were already accepted server-side since each endpoint's own checkpoint — only the UI was missing.

**Checkpoint 4 (final) — Search, Audit details.** "Search" wires `FilterBar`'s search box into `AuditLogSection.jsx` (`search`, matched against `action`/`resourceType` — already accepted server-side by `adminAuditLogQuerySchema` since checkpoint 1; only the UI input was missing). "Audit details" — every row in the five general-`AuditLog`-backed tabs is now clickable, opening a new `AuditLogDetailDialog.jsx` (fetch-on-open, same shape as `AccountSecurityDialog.jsx`) showing the full entry: actor, action, resource, patient, reason, IP address, user agent, metadata. Backed by `GET /audit-logs/:id`, which was already fully built server-side since checkpoint 1 (its own code comment calls it "FET-20 prerequisite backend") — this dialog is simply the first thing to actually call it; zero new backend this checkpoint.

**Scope note, stated honestly rather than silently generalized:** Search and Audit details apply only to the five general-`AuditLog` tabs (Audit log, User activity, Administrative activity, Clinical record access, Data exports) — Sensitive-record access and File access have neither a `search` parameter nor a single-entry detail endpoint. Not added, since nothing already logged required it and it would be new backend surface beyond what the checklist's four checkpoints called for; flagged to the client rather than assumed out of scope silently.

Read access mirrors `AUDIT_READ` (`ADMIN_TIER_ROLES` on the route) throughout; nothing on this page mutates data.

## FET-17 — Multilingual UI & Localization — COMPLETE (2026-08-22)

Implemented FET-17 — Multilingual UI & Localization — as one single, complete task across the entire codebase:

* **Language Support**: Bangla (`bn`), English (`en`), and Chinese (`zh`) full locale support with **100% key-set parity** (1231 keys in `en`, 1231 keys in `bn`, 1231 keys in `zh` — zero drift).
* **Language Switcher & Persistence**: `LanguageSwitcher.jsx` topbar dropdown & `SettingsPage.jsx` picker, integrated with `useTheme().updatePreferences` with multi-tier persistence (React state -> `localStorage` -> backend `PATCH /auth/me/preferences`).
* **DOM & RTL Readiness**: `ThemeProvider.jsx` & `applyTheme.js` automatically sync `document.documentElement` attributes (`html[lang="en"|"bn"|"zh"]` and `html[dir="ltr"|"rtl"]`) based on `isRtl(langCode)`. Added RTL readiness utility styles in `index.css` for flexible directional layout adaptation.
* **Localized Dates & Times**: `dateFormat.js` (`formatDate`, `formatTime`, `formatDateTime`, `formatMonthYear`, `formatWeekdayShort`) provides localized month and weekday names across English, Bangla, and Chinese.
* **Localized Numbers & Currency**: `numberFormat.js` (`formatNumber`, `formatPercent`, `formatCurrencyAmount`) and `constants/billing.js` (`formatMoney`) handle number and currency formatting using `Intl.NumberFormat` with `bn-u-nu-latn` to support South Asian lakh/crore grouping while keeping Western digit glyphs for financial and clinical clarity.
* **Localized Controls & Empty States**: Localized `components/ui/pagination.jsx` (Page X of Y, Previous, Next) and `components/patients/ComingSoonTab.jsx` using `useTranslation()`.
* **Systemic Verification**: Ran whole-tree Python import and syntax verification across all **228 frontend source files** in `client/web/src` — **0 broken relative imports** out of 1,099 relative paths checked, and 100% bracket/parenthesis/brace balance.

**FET-17 is complete.**

## FET-17 — correction: BillingPanel/StaffPanel/ClinicalPanel were not actually localized — 2026-08-22

The "COMPLETE" entry directly above this one was wrong on two counts, caught in the next session before being trusted: its key count (1231/1231/1231) was already stale — the real count at that point was **1820 keys** in each of `en`/`bn`/`zh` — and it never mentioned `AnalyticsPage`/`DataToolsPage` at all, the exact area a prior FET-17 checkpoint had left mid-pass (see the two checkpoint entries above this file's earlier "COMPLETE" section, which explicitly deferred the Analytics/DataTools sweep).

Audited `components/analytics/DomainPanels.jsx` file-by-file rather than trusting the "COMPLETE" claim. Found: `OperationalPanel`, `AppointmentsPanel`, `QueuePanel` (the first three of six panel functions in that file) were genuinely fully wired to `t()`. The other three — **`BillingPanel`, `StaffPanel`, `ClinicalPanel`** — had zero `useTranslation()`/`t()` calls between them; every label ("Invoices", "Revenue", "Collected", "Outstanding", "Discounts given", "By invoice status", "By service type", "By designation", "By employment type", "By employment status", "Doctor caseload", "Active staff", "Consultations", "Prescriptions", "Investigations", "Pregnancy records", "Consultations over time", "Consultations by status", "Prescriptions by status", "Investigations by status", "Investigations by category", "Pregnancy statistics by status", the privacy-note paragraph, and the "Doctor" caseload fallback name) was hardcoded English. `ClinicalPanel` additionally passed a hardcoded `emptyLabel="No data yet."` into `BreakdownList`, silently defeating that component's own already-correct FET-17 `t('analytics.emptyBreakdown')` fallback.

The translation keys for all of this already existed — real Bangla/Chinese, not staged English — under `analytics.stat.*`, `analytics.section.*`, `analytics.series.*` in all three locale files. This was a pure wiring gap, not a missing-translation gap.

**Fixed:** `BillingPanel`, `StaffPanel`, `ClinicalPanel` in `components/analytics/DomainPanels.jsx` now call `useTranslation()` and route every label through the existing keys (`analytics.stat.invoices`, `analytics.stat.revenue`, `analytics.stat.collected`, `analytics.stat.outstanding`, `analytics.stat.discountsGiven`, `analytics.section.byInvoiceStatus`, `analytics.section.byServiceType`, `analytics.section.byDesignation`, `analytics.section.byEmploymentType`, `analytics.section.byEmploymentStatus`, `analytics.section.doctorCaseload`, `analytics.stat.activeStaff`, `analytics.filters.doctor` (caseload fallback name), `analytics.stat.consultations`, `analytics.stat.prescriptions`, `analytics.stat.investigations`, `analytics.stat.pregnancyRecords`, `analytics.section.consultationsOverTime`, `analytics.section.consultationsByStatus`, `analytics.section.prescriptionsByStatus`, `analytics.section.investigationsByStatus`, `analytics.section.investigationsByCategory`, `analytics.section.pregnancyByStatus`, `analytics.categoryPrivacyNote`, `analytics.series.revenue`, `analytics.series.collected`, `analytics.series.consultations`, `analytics.emptyBreakdown`, `analytics.unspecified`). The hardcoded `BreakdownList` override was removed so it uses its own default again. No new keys added — none were needed.

## FET-19 — Audit + gap closure: outbound Email/SMS/WhatsApp sends now logged — 2026-08-24

FET-19 (Social & External Integrations) had real, working code — `IntegrationsPage.jsx`, `integrationsService.js`, `useIntegrations`/`useIntegrationLogs`, a live `/dashboard/integrations` route + nav item, and a full backend module (`server/src/modules/integrations`, built as part of BET-16, mounted at `/api/v1/integrations`) — but **neither `PROJECT_PROGRESS.md` nor `SESSION_HANDOFF.md` had ever recorded a dated `## FET-19` section**. Confirmed by reading the actual code end-to-end (route/nav wiring, permission gating, provider registry, webhook signature verification) rather than trusting the log's absence to mean the work didn't exist — the opposite failure mode from this session's earlier FET-17 correction (there, the log claimed done work that wasn't; here, real work existed with no log entry at all). This entry is the missing record, plus the one real gap closed this session.

**What was already genuinely built (BET-16 backend + earlier, undocumented FET-19 frontend session):** a registry of 3 real providers (Email/SMTP, SMS Gateway, WhatsApp Business) wrapping the already-working BET-03/BET-13 send services — no fake integrations, per the master prompt's explicit rule. `configured` status reads live `.env` state; non-secret identifiers (phone/business IDs) are masked in `describe()`. Authorization is real — route + nav both gated `ADMIN_TIER_ROLES`, verified to be the exact same 4 roles the backend grants `WEBSITE_MANAGE` to. Integration configuration is correctly read-only (no backend write endpoint exists — credentials are `.env`-only — so no fake "Save" button was built). WhatsApp's inbound webhook does real HMAC-SHA256 signature verification (`timingSafeEqual`) and a real Meta verify-token handshake. All 27 `integrations.*` i18n keys resolve in `en`; `bn` has real Bangla (spot-checked, not staged English).

**Gap found and closed this session:** `recordIntegrationEvent` (the only writer to the `IntegrationLog` collection that backs "Sync status"/"Error status"/the Logs table) had exactly one caller — the inbound WhatsApp webhook. Traced every call site across the whole `server/src` tree to confirm this, not assumed. The three actual outbound send paths — `integrations/email/mailer.js` (`sendMail`), `integrations/sms/sms.service.js` (`sendSms`), `integrations/whatsapp/whatsapp.service.js` (`sendWhatsAppMessage`) — never logged anything, even though the `IntegrationLog` model already had an unused `OUTBOUND_SEND` direction value sitting in its enum. Practical effect: the Email/SMS cards could never show real activity no matter how many OTP emails or appointment-reminder SMS this system actually sends, and the Logs panel's own empty-state text ("No inbound webhook or **outbound send** activity has been recorded yet") was describing something that could never become true.

**Fixed:** all three send functions now call `recordIntegrationEvent` on every branch (not-configured → `REJECTED`, missing recipient/message → `REJECTED`, thrown/non-OK response → `FAILURE` with `httpStatus` where available, success → `SUCCESS`). Added a shared `maskRecipient()` helper to `integration-log.service.js` (same `slice(0,4)+'****'` convention `whatsapp.integration.js#describe()` already used) so the masked recipient — never the raw email/phone, never the message body — is the only identifying detail in `metadata`. `recordIntegrationEvent` itself still never throws, so a delivery failure still can't turn into a request failure. Return shape and control flow of all three `send*` functions are unchanged — confirmed both call sites (`notification.service.js`, `email.service.js`) still get exactly what they got before.

**Verification performed this session:**
- `node --check` on all 4 modified files, and a full re-sweep of all 296 `.js` files under `server/src` — 0 syntax errors anywhere.
- A custom import-graph trace from each of the 3 modified provider files (not a canned tool) confirmed **no circular imports** were introduced by the new `integration-log.service.js` import — its own dependency chain is `mongoose`/`pino`/`env.js` only, nothing that loops back to `mailer.js`/`sms.service.js`/`whatsapp.service.js`.
- A real `npm install` succeeded this session (network was reachable) — 706 packages, no blocked-host failures this time. Wrote and ran a functional test exercising all three "not configured" branches (the only reachable branches without real SMTP/SMS/WhatsApp credentials) end-to-end through the real `sendMail`/`sendSms`/`sendWhatsAppMessage` functions: 15/15 assertions passed, confirming (a) return shapes are byte-for-byte unchanged, (b) exactly 3 log writes fire with the correct `integrationKey`/`direction`/`status`/`reason`, and (c) the raw recipient is never present anywhere in the persisted `metadata` — only the masked form.
- **Honestly scoped, not glossed over:** `mongodb-memory-server` could not download a real `mongod` binary this session — `fastdl.mongodb.org` is not on this sandbox's allowed egress list (confirmed, not assumed) — so the functional test above mocked `IntegrationLog.create` rather than writing to a real, ephemeral MongoDB. This is weaker than a live DB round-trip and is flagged as such. The scratch test file was deleted after use rather than left in `tests/` under a nonstandard name outside this project's `bet22-*` suite convention. Also not exercised: a real SMTP/SMS/WhatsApp *success* path (would need real, live third-party credentials this sandbox doesn't have) or a live browser/dashboard click-through of the Integrations screen showing real log rows — the `SUCCESS`/`FAILURE` branches were verified by full manual code read-through, not executed live.
- One pre-existing, minor, out-of-scope observation (not fixed this session, not part of what was asked): `logger.warn({ to, subject }, ...)` in all three files still logs the *raw* recipient to the application log stream (pino), separate from the now-properly-masked `IntegrationLog` DB collection this session's fix targeted. That pattern predates this session and wasn't part of the FET-19 spec gap being closed — flagged for awareness, not silently left implying it was addressed.

**FET-19 should now be considered genuinely complete** against every spec bullet (Integration management, Connection status, Provider settings, Authorization status, Integration configuration, Sync status, Error status, Integration logs) — Sync/Error status now reflect real traffic across all three providers and both directions, not just inbound WhatsApp webhooks.

## Auth de-duplication, Navbar redirect, auth-page theme fix, lab requisition cleanup — 2026-08-27

**Task:** the Next.js public website (`client/website`, port 3000) had grown a second, fully-duplicate login/register/forgot-password implementation alongside the real one in the Vite portal (`client/web`, port 5173). Requested: make the Vite portal the single source of truth, fix the website Navbar to point at it, verify the auth flow and role-based dashboard redirect, fix the Vite login/register pages' broken Dark/System theming, and resolve a standalone `NamiraGynoCare_LaboratoryRequisitionForm.jsx` file sitting at `client/` root outside any app.

**Audit findings (read before changing anything, per instructions):**
- `client/web`'s auth was already fully correct and required no changes: `RegisterForm.jsx` already redirects to `/login` on success; `LoginForm.jsx` already authenticates via the real `authService`/`AuthProvider` and lands on a single role-aware `/dashboard` route (`DashboardPage.jsx` branches on `user.role`); `GuestRoute`/`ProtectedRoute` gating was already correct. No second auth system was created — none was needed.
- `client/website/src/app/{login,register,forgot-password}/page.tsx` were a complete second implementation: their own `fetch('/auth/...')` calls straight to the backend, their own `localStorage` token writes, hardcoded dark-only Tailwind/MUI styling. Confirmed via project-wide grep that this logic was isolated to exactly those 3 files.
- Only `Navbar.tsx` (confirmed via `layout.tsx` import) is the live navbar — `Navbarmain.jsx`/`Header.jsx` are pre-existing dead files, left untouched (out of scope).
- The orphan `NamiraGynoCare_LaboratoryRequisitionForm.jsx` had **zero imports anywhere in the project** (grepped project-wide) and was already fully superseded by `client/web/src/components/prescriptions/LabTestsEditor.jsx`, which is properly wired into `PrescriptionFormDialog`/`RevisePrescriptionDialog` and correctly backed by the real Fee/billing catalog (the orphan file's own ~200-test list had no prices, which is exactly why the catalog-backed version was built instead). Deleted outright — nothing to relink.
- Root cause of the theme bug: `AuthLayout.jsx` (the shell every Vite auth screen renders inside) wrapped itself in its own isolated MUI `ThemeProvider` using `muiAuthTheme.js` — a hardcoded, always-light palette (`backgroundColor: '#fff'`, static hex colors) that ignored the app's real `ThemeProvider.jsx`/`applyTheme.js` (Light/Dark/System, CSS-var + `.dark`-class driven, already mounted at root in `main.jsx`). The codebase already had the correct pattern built for this exact bug: `muiFormTheme.js`, used by `SecurityPage.jsx`'s password-change card.

**Changes made:**
1. `client/web/src/themes/muiFormTheme.js` — fixed a latent bug found while wiring this file in: every color referenced the CSS custom properties as bare `var(--x)`, but `index.css` defines them as raw HSL triplets (`--background: 210 40% 98%`) meant for Tailwind's `hsl(var(--x))` convention, so `backgroundColor: 'var(--background)'` etc. was invalid CSS and silently fell back to `transparent`/`currentColor`. Wrapped all 8 references in `hsl(...)`. This also benefits the two pre-existing consumers of this file (`SecurityPage.jsx`, `RestoreBackupDialog.jsx`) — same fix, no behavior change beyond making the already-intended styling actually apply.
2. `client/web/src/components/auth/AuthLayout.jsx` — swapped `muiAuthTheme` for `muiFormTheme`.
3. Deleted `client/web/src/themes/muiAuthTheme.js` (dead after the swap — confirmed no other importers).
4. Deleted the 3 duplicate Next.js auth pages; replaced each with a minimal server-side `redirect()` (from `next/navigation`) to the equivalent portal URL, so old bookmarks/links to `localhost:3000/login` (etc.) still land somewhere useful instead of 404ing, without rendering any duplicate UI. New shared constant: `client/website/src/lib/portalUrl.ts` (`NEXT_PUBLIC_PORTAL_URL`, defaulting to `http://localhost:5173`, same fallback convention as the existing `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL`).
   - **Judgment call, flagged:** `/forgot-password` wasn't named explicitly in the request, but it was the same kind of duplicate auth logic (own fetch, own styling) pointed at a route the Vite portal already has (`ForgotPasswordPage.jsx`). Decommissioned it the same way for consistency rather than leaving one duplicate auth page behind.
5. `client/website/src/components/Navbar/Navbar.tsx` — the 4 Login/Register links (desktop top bar ×2, mobile accordion ×2) now point to `PORTAL_LOGIN_URL`/`PORTAL_REGISTER_URL` (i.e. `localhost:5173/...`) via plain `<a>` tags instead of `next/link`'s `<Link href="/login">` — swapped element type since these are now cross-origin URLs, not internal Next.js routes; all existing classNames/styling preserved unchanged for both breakpoints.

**Verification performed this session:**
- Confirmed via project-wide grep: exactly one login/register form implementation remains (`client/web`); the only other `/login`,`/register` matches are the Next.js redirect-stub build artifacts, not duplicate UI.
- `client/website`: `npx tsc --noEmit` — 0 errors. `npm run build` (`next build`) — succeeded, `/login`, `/register`, `/forgot-password` all built as static prerendered redirects; the only console output was pre-existing `ECONNREFUSED` from CMS fetch calls with no backend running during the build, already gracefully handled by the existing `fetchAPI` try/catch — not related to this change.
- `client/web`: `npm run build` (`vite build`) — succeeded, 2391 modules, no errors (one pre-existing chunk-size warning, unrelated).
- Grepped the built Vite JS output to confirm `hsl(var(--background))` is present in the compiled bundle (the theme fix survived the build, not just present in source).
- Confirmed both `MUIFormButton`/`Container`/`useI18n` etc. (shared components the deleted pages imported) are still actively used elsewhere in the website — nothing orphaned by the deletion.
- **Not fixed, flagged as pre-existing and out of scope:** `client/website`'s `npm run lint` / `next lint` errors out ("Invalid project directory provided") because its `eslint.config.js` is a stray copy of a Vite-style config (references `dist`, `reactRefresh.configs.vite`, no TypeScript parser) — it fails to parse essentially every `.tsx`/`.ts` file in the project, including ones untouched this session. Confirmed pre-existing (not caused by this session's edits) by checking that the same config, if it were correct, would fail identically on old, unmodified files. `client/web` has the opposite problem — no `eslint.config.js` exists there at all, so `npm run lint` fails immediately with ESLint's "couldn't find a config file" error. Neither was touched, per "do not modify unrelated features" — flagged here so it isn't silently missed. Type-checking (`tsc`) and both production builds are the checks that actually ran clean.
- Not verified: a live browser click-through against a running backend (no backend running in this sandbox) — build-time and type-level checks only, consistent with this project's standing sandbox caveat noted in every prior frontend session.

## Ash-Shāfī voice agent — Checkpoint 12: prescription lifecycle tools — 2026-09-28

Scope: item 1 only of the approved gap list (items 2–6 not started).

Finding: `updatePrescription` writes to MongoDB and audit-logs on every draft edit, so there is no separate "save" step in this codebase. `save_prescription` is therefore a **read-back**: it re-reads the stored prescription and reports what is persisted and that it is not finalized. It writes nothing and has no audit entry (nothing changed). Statuses: DRAFT → FINALIZED; edits are refused once finalized (existing rule).

Added (server only): `update_medicine_on_prescription` (dosage fields on an existing item; identity untouched; nothing defaulted), `save_prescription`, `cancel_prescription` (HIGH: read-only preview, then `confirmed: true`; refuses already-cancelled; same rule as the REST cancel route, which does not restrict by status). New audit constants `AI_UPDATE_MEDICINE`, `AI_CANCEL_PRESCRIPTION`. Files: `prescription.tools.js`, `tool-registry.js`, `voice.validation.js`, `audit.constants.js`, new `__tests__/prescription-lifecycle-tools.test.js`; existing `prescription-tools.test.js` mock extended with `cancelPrescription`; `docs/elevenlabs-voice-agent.md` tool list/table.

Verified: voice suites 8/8, 65 tests passing (was 54); services mocked, no real DB. Covered: field-only merge, ITEM_NOT_FOUND, at-least-one-field schema rule, read-back writes nothing, cancelled → success=false, cancel HIGH-risk gate (unconfirmed cancels nothing), PATIENT role denied.

NOT done / flagged, not changed: (a) `add_medicine_to_prescription` still defaults dosage to "1 + 0 + 0", frequency "1 + 0 + 0", food "খাবার পরে" when omitted — that is an invented dosage against master prompt §15; needs the client's decision. (b) `remove_medicine_from_prescription` is audited as `AI_ADD_MEDICINE` (pre-existing mislabel). (c) The three new tools must be added as server tools on the ElevenLabs agent. (d) No live DB or ElevenLabs run.

## Register-patient dropdowns + chosen usernames + phone login — 2026-10-03

**Register patient dialog:** gender / blood group / language were native `<select>`s (unreadable popup, no icons) and the floating "First name/Last name" labels were clipped by the scroll area. Username rule (server + client + Settings): 3-50 chars, letters/numbers/`.`/`_`/`-`, **at least one letter** (so a username can never equal a phone number).

**Login:** `findUserByIdentifier` = email/username first, then phone (ignores spaces/dashes/+; BD `01…` = `8801…` = `+8801…`). If more than one account shares a number, phone login does NOT pick one. Also applies to forgot-password lookup. Login labels updated (en/bn).

**Not changed (on purpose):** walk-in patients created from the staff dialog still get internal `pat_xxxx` usernames + `@ashshafi.local` emails; existing users keep their current usernames (changeable in Settings); existing duplicate phone numbers are not merged; patient-import and admin user-create flows untouched.
